taking values and in
\mathcal{S}_{n_{0}}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying Assumptions
4.2
, each
i_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,n_{0}\}
and
\chi_{n_{0}}\in H^{1}(\nu)
, the solution
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
of

(4.30)

\begin{cases}\mathsf{M}{\psi}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}{\psi}=\chi_{n_{0}}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on $\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}$)}\\
{\psi}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on $\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})$}\end{cases}

satisfies for some
\alpha\geq s-\frac{1}{2}
,
\kappa>0
and
\delta>0
the estimate

(4.31)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{1+d(j,i_{0})^{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{n}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n_{0}}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We wish to prove that (
4.31
) holds for
\alpha=s
. Without loss of generality one may assume that
n=n_{0}
. Fix
i_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}(\nu)
and
\psi
solution of (
4.30
).

Step 2: localization

Fix an index
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and define the window

(4.32)

J=\{i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}:d(j,i)\leq d(j,i_{0})/2\}.

Our aim is to study the equation satisfied by
\psi^{J}:=(\psi_{j})_{j\in J}\in L^{2}(J,H^{1}(\nu))
. Projecting Equation (
5.1
) on the
l
-th coordinate for
l\in J
reads, since
i_{0}\notin J
,

\beta\sum_{i\in J}\mathsf{M}_{i,l}\psi_{i}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi_{l}=-\beta\sum_{i\in J^{c}}\mathsf{M}_{i,l}\psi_{i}.

Let us denote
\mathsf{M}^{J}=(\mathsf{M}_{i,j})_{i,j\in J}
and
\mathrm{V}\in L^{2}(J,H^{1}(\nu))
given for each
l\in J
by

(4.33)

\mathrm{V}_{l}=-\beta\sum_{i\in J^{c}}\mathsf{M}_{i,l}\psi_{i},

so that
\psi^{J}
solves

(4.34)

\begin{cases}\beta\mathsf{M}^{J}\psi^{J}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi^{J}=\mathrm{V}&\text{on $\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}$)}\\
\psi^{J}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on $\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})$}.\end{cases}

Step 3: bound on the exterior field

Fix
l\in J
and split
\mathrm{V}_{l}
into

(4.35)

\mathrm{V}_{l}=\underbrace{\sum_{i\in J^{c},d(i,i_{0})\leq\frac{1}{2}d(j,i_{0})}\mathsf{M}_{i,l}\psi_{i}}_{({I})_{l}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i\in J^{c},d(i,i_{0})>\frac{1}{2}d(j,i_{0})}\mathsf{M}_{i,l}\psi_{i}}_{({II})_{l}}.

Using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Lemma
4.2
, we find

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[({I})_{l}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\frac{1}{d(j,i_{0})^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{1}{d(l,\partial J)^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

On the other hand using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Lemma
4.2
again, one gets

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[({II})_{l}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(j,i_{0})^{s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Step 4: optimal decay for the auxiliary system

Let us split
\psi=\sum_{l\in J}\psi^{(l)}
, where for each
l\in J

\psi^{(l)}\in L^{2}(J,H^{1}(\nu))
solves

\begin{cases}\beta\mathsf{M}^{J}\psi^{(l)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi^{(l)}=\mathrm{V}_{l}e_{l}&\text{on $\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}$)}\\
\psi^{(l)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on $\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})$}\end{cases}

One may apply the bootstrap assumption (
4.31
) to
\mathsf{M}^{J}
and
\psi^{(l)}
, which gives the bound

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\psi^{(l)}_{j})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\frac{1}{d(j,l)^{\alpha}}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d(j,i_{0})^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{1}{d(l,\partial J)^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{1}{d(j,i_{0})^{s}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Summing this over
l\in J
yields

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(j,i_{0})^{\alpha^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

where

\alpha^{\prime}=\min(s,s+\alpha-1,3s-\alpha).

Since
\alpha\geq s-\frac{1}{2}
and
s>1
,
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha
. After a finite number of iterations, we find that (
4.31
) holds for
\alpha=s
.
∎

4.5.
Conclusion in the case
n=N

In view of Proposition
3.3
, the H.-S. equation contains when
n=N
a Lagrange multiplier associated to the linear constraints that
y_{1}+\ldots+y_{N}=N
on
\mathcal{M}_{N}
. Controlling this multiplier entails the following:

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
s\in(1,+\infty).
Let
\nu
and
\mathsf{M}
satisfying Assumptions
4.1
and
4.2
. Let
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}(\nu),i_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Let
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
solution of

(4.36)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}\psi+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{n})&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{n})=0&\text{on $\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})$}\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Let
d
be as in (
4.19
). There exist constants
C(\beta)>0,\delta>0
such that for each
1\leq j\leq n
,

(4.37)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{1+d(j,i_{0})^{s}}+\frac{1}{n}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

Let us first prove that the Lagrange multiplier
\lambda
in (
4.36
) satisfies

(4.38)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{1-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

for some constants
C(\beta)>0,\kappa>0
. By linearity one can split
\psi
into
\psi=\psi^{(1)}+\psi^{(2)}
where
\psi^{(1)}\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
solves

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}\psi^{(1)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi^{(1)}=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi^{(1)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

In view of Proposition
4.3
, we have that uniformly in
j\in I
,

(4.39)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\psi^{(1)}_{j})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\frac{1}{1+d(j,i_{0})^{s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

(4.40)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\psi^{(2)}_{j})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Let
K_{0}\geq 1
. Split
\mathsf{M}
into
\mathsf{M}^{(1)}+\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
with
\mathsf{M}^{(1)}
given for each
i,j\in I
by

\mathsf{M}^{(1)}_{i,j}=\mathsf{M}_{i,j}\mathds{1}_{d(i,j)\leq K_{0}}.

Let
u=\sum_{j\in I}e_{j}
. Recall from (
3.10
) that

n\lambda=\beta u\cdot\mathsf{M}\psi-\chi_{n}=\beta u\cdot\mathsf{M}^{(1)}\psi+\beta u\cdot\mathsf{M}^{(2)}\psi-\chi_{n}.

First note that there exists
C(\beta)>0,\kappa>0
such that

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(u\cdot\mathsf{M}^{(1)}\psi)^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}K_{0}^{\kappa}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Moreover taking the scalar product of (
4.36
) with
\psi
and integrating by parts under
\nu
yields the energetic estimate

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Consequently there exists constants
C(\beta)>0,\kappa>0
such that

(4.41)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(u\cdot\mathsf{M}^{(1)}\psi)^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}K_{0}^{\kappa}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Besides, employing (
4.39
), we find

(4.42)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(u\cdot\mathsf{M}^{(2)}\psi^{(1)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Finally, note

|u\cdot\mathsf{M}^{(2)}\psi^{(2)}|\leq\sum_{i,k:d(i,k)\geq K_{0}}|\mathsf{M}_{i,k}||\psi_{k}^{(2)}|\leq n^{\kappa\varepsilon}K_{0}^{-s}\sum_{k}|\psi_{k}^{(2)}|.

Using the bound (
4.40
) one can see that

(4.43)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(u\cdot\mathsf{M}^{(2)}\psi^{(2)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}K_{0}^{-s}n\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Taking
K_{0}
large with enough with respect to
n^{\varepsilon}
, one can make the left-hand side of (
4.43
) smaller than
\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Combining this with (
4.41
) and (
4.42
) one obtains

n\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

which proves (
4.38
). Combined with (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) this concludes the proof of (
4.37
).
∎

4.6.
Estimate on the main equation

There remains to compare the solution of (
4.1
) to the solution
\psi^{(1)}
of the simplified equation (
4.20
). This supposes to estimate the quantity
M\psi^{(1)}
where
M
is the perturbation in (
4.17
).

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
s\in(1,+\infty)
Let
\nu
be the measure (
4.11
). Let
i_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
such that
|i_{0}-n/2|\leq n/4
,
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}(\nu)
and
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

(4.44)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A_{1}^{\nu}\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Let
d
be as in (
4.19
). Then, uniformly in
1\leq j\leq n
, we have

(4.45)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{1+d(i_{0},j)^{s}}+\frac{1}{n^{\min(s-1/2,2s-2)}}\Bigr)(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\chi_{n}|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}).

Similarly let
w\in L^{2}(\{1,\ldots,N\},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

(4.46)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\nabla^{2}({\mathcal{H}}_{N}^{\mathrm{g}}+\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}})w+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}w=\chi_{N}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{N})&\text{on }\mathcal{M}_{N}\\
w\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{N})=0&\text{on }\mathcal{M}_{N}\\
w\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\mathcal{M}_{N}\end{array}\right.

Then, uniformly in
1\leq j\leq N
, we have

(4.47)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{1+d(i_{0},j)^{s}}+\frac{1}{N}\Bigr)(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\chi_{n}|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}).

Proof.

Let us prove the bound (
4.45
). Let
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of (
4.44
),
\psi^{(1)}\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
solving

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}\psi^{(1)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi^{(1)}=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi^{(1)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Define
\psi^{(2)}=\psi-\psi^{(1)}
. One can check that
\psi^{(2)}
is solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A_{1}^{\nu}\psi^{(2)}=-\beta M^{(1)}\psi^{(1)}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi^{(2)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Denote
M^{(1)}=\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}-A\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}
and
M^{(2)}=\nabla^{2}\widetilde{E}
. One gets by integration by parts

(4.48)

\beta\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi^{(2)}\cdot\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{g}}_{n}\psi^{(2)}]\\
\leq C(\beta)\left(\sup|\psi^{(1)}|(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|M^{(1)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}|M^{(2)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}})+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}|M^{(2)}\psi^{(1)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{(2)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The maximum principle of Proposition
3.6
gives the existence of
C(\beta),\kappa>0
such that

\sup|\psi^{(1)}|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\sup|\chi_{n}|.

Moreover, using Assumption
4.1
, one finds that there exist constants
C(\beta)>0,c(\beta)>0,\delta>0
such that

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|M^{(1)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}},

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}|M^{(2)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}.

Let us now estimate the vector-field
M^{(2)}\psi^{(1)}
. We claim that for uniformly in
1\leq j\leq n
,

(4.49)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(M^{(2)}\psi^{(1)})_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{1+d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\frac{1}{n^{s-1/2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Fix
1\leq j\leq n
. Recall that for any
x
in the interior of
\mathcal{A}
and for each
1\leq k,l\leq n
,

M_{k,l}^{(2)}(x)=\partial_{kl}\widetilde{E}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid x)}[\partial_{kl}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}]-\mathrm{Cov}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid x)}[\partial_{k}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}},\partial_{l}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}].

In view of (
4.18
) we have that for each
1\leq k,l\leq n
,

(4.50)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(M_{k,l}^{(2)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{1+d(k,\partial I)^{s-1/2}d(l,\partial I)^{s-1/2}}.

One can then split the quantity
(M^{(2)}\psi^{(1)})_{j}
into

(M^{(2)}\psi^{(1)})_{j}=\underbrace{\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)\leq n/4}M_{j,k}^{(2)}\psi_{k}^{(1)}}_{({I})_{j}}+\underbrace{\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)>n/4}M_{j,k}^{(2)}\psi_{k}^{(1)}}_{({II})_{j}}.

For the first quantity, using (
4.50
) and (
4.29
), we can write

(4.51)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}({I})_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}&\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{1+d(j,\partial I)^{s-1/2}}\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)\leq n/4}\frac{1}{|k-\frac{n}{2}|^{s}}\frac{1}{1+d(k,\partial I)^{s-1/2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{1+d(j,\partial I)^{s-1/2}}\frac{1}{n^{\min(s,2s-3/2)}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

For the second quantity using the bound on the increments of
M
given in (
4.18
), we find

(4.52)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}\Bigr(\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)>n/4}M_{j,k}^{(2)}\psi_{k}^{(1)}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{1+d(j,\partial I)^{s-1/2}}\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)>n/4}\frac{1}{|k-\frac{n}{2}|^{s}}\frac{1}{n^{s-1/2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{1+d(j,\partial I)^{s-1/2}}\frac{1}{n^{s-1/2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Putting (
4.51
) and (
4.52
) together we obtain (
4.49
). Summing this over
j
yields

(4.53)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|M^{(2)}\psi^{(1)}|^{2}]^{1/2}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{n^{\min(s-1/2,2s-2)}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using the uniform convexity of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}
, we then obtain from (
4.48
) the bound

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{(2)}|^{2}]^{1/2}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{n^{\min(s-1/2,2s-2)}}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\chi_{n}|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}).

In particular, together with (
4.29
), this yields (
4.45
). The proof of (
4.47
) follows from similar considerations by making use of Lemma
4.4
.
∎

4.7.
Decay of gaps correlations

We are now ready to conclude the proof of the decay of correlations for the hypersingular Riesz gas. When
x_{i}
and
x_{j}
are at macroscopic or large mesoscopic distance, one can take
n=N
and use the estimate of Proposition
4.3
. Otherwise one may choose
n
to be a power of
d_{N}(i,j)
and apply the estimate of Proposition
4.5
for such a number
n
, thus completing the proof of Theorem
2
.

Proof of of Theorem
2
.

Let
\nu
be the constrained measure on
\{1,\ldots,N\}
defined in (
4.11
) with
n=N
. Using the Pinsker inequality, the fact that
\nu
satisfies a Log-Sobolev inequality (see Lemma
3.8
) and the local law of Lemma
C.1
, one can observe that

\mathrm{TV}(\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}},\nu)\leq(2\mathrm{Ent}(\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\mid\nu))^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)N^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[|\nabla\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)N^{\delta}}.

In particular, it follows that

(4.54)

\mathrm{Cov}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}}[\xi(N(x_{j+1}-x_{j})),\chi(N(x_{i+1}-x_{i}))]=\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu}[\xi(x_{j}),\chi(x_{i})]+O_{\beta}(e^{-N^{\delta}}\sup|\xi|\sup|\chi|).

Moreover, by Proposition
3.3
, the covariance term in the last display may be expressed as

\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu}[\xi(x_{j}),\chi(x_{i})]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\xi^{\prime}(x_{j})\partial_{j}\phi],

with
\nabla\phi\in L^{2}(\{1,\ldots,N\},H^{1}(\nu))
solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A_{1}^{\nu}\nabla\phi=\chi^{\prime}(x_{i})e_{i}+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{N})&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\nabla\phi\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{N})=0&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\nabla\phi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Using the estimate of Proposition
4.5
, Hölder’s inequality and (
4.54
), one obtains that there exists some small
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that (
1.5
) holds in the case where
d_{N}(i,j)\geq N^{\varepsilon_{0}}
.

We now consider the case where
d_{N}(i,j)
is smaller than
N^{\varepsilon_{0}}
. Let
n\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
be the smallest number such that

n^{-\min(s-1/2,2s-2)}\leq{d_{N}(i,j)^{-(1+s)}}.

Without loss of generality, one can assume that
1\leq\frac{n}{3}\leq i,j\leq\frac{2n}{3}
. Since
N(x_{i+1}-x_{i})
and
N(x_{j+1}-x_{1})
are functions of
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
and since
\mathcal{A}
has overwhelming probability, one may write

(4.55)

\mathrm{Cov}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}}[\xi(N(x_{j+1}-x_{j})),\chi(N(x_{i+1}-x_{i}))]=\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu}[\xi(x_{j}),\chi(x_{i})]+O_{\beta}(e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\sup|\xi|\sup|\chi|).

By Proposition
3.3
again one can express this covariance term as

(4.56)

\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu}[x_{j},x_{i}]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\xi^{\prime}(x_{j})\partial_{j}\phi],

where
\nabla\phi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
is solution of

(4.57)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A_{1}^{\nu}\nabla\phi=\chi^{\prime}(x_{i})e_{i}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\nabla\phi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Inserting the result of Proposition
4.5
we find that

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\partial_{j}\phi)^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{N}(i,j)^{s}}+\frac{1}{n^{\min(s-1/2,2s-2)}}\Bigr)(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi^{\prime}(x_{i})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\chi^{\prime}|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}).

Inserting this into (
4.56
) and using (
4.55
) completes the proof of (
1.5
) by choosing
n
large enough.
∎

5.
Decay of correlations for the long-range Riesz gas

This section is the core of the paper and aims to develop a method to study the decay of correlations in the long-range case
s\in(0,1)
. Because the Hessian of the energy in gap coordinates concentrates around the matrix (
2.5
) which has slowly decaying entries, it is not clear how the strategy of Section
4
can be adapted. Indeed the commutation result of Lemma
4.1
cannot be applied to (
2.5
). The trick is to exploit the fact that the Hessian is not only positive-definite but actually controls a fractional primitive of the solution. This should be compared with the method of
[
Bou21
, Sec. 4]
adapted from
[
BEY12
, Sec. 3]
which exploits the long-range nature of the interaction to have sharp concentration estimates.

5.1.
Periodization

Let us begin by performing the following series of reductions, leading to the study of a simplified equation:

(1)

Convexification and reduction to a smaller window,

(2)

Adding of a Schur complement to the energy of the
n
points and splitting of the H.-S. operator,

(3)

Embedding the system into a periodic system of
\bar{n}\gg n
points,

(4)

Control on the perturbation operator.

As pointed out in Section
4
the study of the correlations at microscopic distance requires to localize the system at a smaller scale. Let
n\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
be the active scale with
n=N
or
n\leq\frac{N}{2}
,
I
the window
I=\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\pi:\mathcal{M}_{N}\to\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be the projection on the coordinates
(x_{i})_{i\in I}
. Let
\theta:\mathbb{R}^{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
smooth such that
\theta=0
on
(0,\frac{1}{2})
,
\theta^{\prime\prime}\geq 1
on
(1,+\infty)
and
\theta^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
on
[0,+\infty]
. Let
\varepsilon>0
and
\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}
be the forcing

\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}:X_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta(n^{-\varepsilon}x_{i})

and the constrained measure

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\propto e^{-\beta\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi}\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}.

Let
\nu=\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi^{-1}
. We will be studying the solution
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
of

(5.1)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A_{1}^{\nu}\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}),\end{array}\right.

In the case where
n\leq\frac{N}{2}
, one would prefer working with a periodic system of size
\bar{n}=\lfloor n^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{s}}\rfloor
instead of (
5.1
). The idea is to subtract from
A_{1}^{\nu}
the appropriate quantity to identify the equation with the projection on the coordinates
(x_{i})_{i\in I}
of a larger system of size
\bar{n}
. Let
\bar{n}=\lfloor n^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{s}}\rfloor
if
n\leq\frac{N}{2}
and
\bar{n}=N
if
n=N
. Set
\bar{I}=\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
. Fix a positive parameter
\varepsilon>0
. Let
K_{0}
be a large power of
\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor.
Define
\mathrm{H}\in\mathcal{M}_{\bar{n}}(\mathbb{R})
the truncated Riesz matrix at distance
K_{0}
, i.e
\mathrm{H}=\nabla^{2}F(x)
for some
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}
where

F:X_{\bar{n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}\mapsto\sum_{i,j\in\bar{I}:d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\geq K_{0}}g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime\prime}(j-i)(x_{i}+\ldots+x_{j})^{2}.

Consider the block decomposition of
\mathrm{H}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}-n}
,

(5.2)

\mathrm{H}=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix},\quad A\in\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathbb{R}).

Also let

G:X_{\bar{n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}\mapsto\sum_{i\in I,j\in I^{c}:d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\geq K_{0}}g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime\prime}(j-i)(x_{i}+\ldots+x_{j})^{2}.

Let
\mathrm{H}^{(2)}=\nabla^{2}G(x)
for some
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}
and
\mathrm{H}^{(1)}=\mathrm{H}-\mathrm{H}^{(2)}
. Consider the block decomposition of
\mathrm{H}^{(1)}
and
\mathrm{H}^{(2)}
:

(5.3)

\mathrm{H}^{(1)}=\begin{pmatrix}A^{(1)}&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{H}^{(2)}=\begin{pmatrix}A^{(2)}&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}.

Since
\mathrm{H}^{(2)}\geq 0
we have
A^{(2)}-BD^{-1}C\geq 0
. Furthermore we also have
A^{(1)}\geq 0
. Noting that
D
is positive-definite, one may consider

B(D+\beta^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}C.

One may now split
A_{1}^{\nu}
. Recall that the measure
\nu
can be written

\mathrm{d}\nu(x)=\exp(-\beta\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}(x))\mathds{1}_{\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})}(x)\mathrm{d}x,

where for any
x\in\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})
and
1\leq i,j\leq n
,

(5.4)

\partial_{ij}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{g}}_{n}(x)=\partial_{ij}\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}(x)+\partial_{ij}\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}(x)+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid x)}[\partial_{ij}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,\cdot)]-\mathrm{Cov}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid x)}[\partial_{i}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,\cdot),\partial_{j}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,\cdot)],

with

(5.5)

\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}:(x,y)\in(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{N-n})\cap\mathcal{M}_{N}\mapsto\mathcal{H}_{N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,y)-\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}(x)-\mathcal{H}_{N-n}^{\mathrm{g}}(y).

Define the good event

(5.6)

\mathcal{A}=\{X_{n}\in\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}):\forall i,i+k\in\{1,\ldots,n\},n^{-\varepsilon}\leq x_{i}\leq n^{\varepsilon},|x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}-k|\leq n^{\varepsilon}k^{\frac{s}{2}}\}.

Let us split
A_{1}^{\nu}
into

(5.7)

A_{1}^{\nu}=\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu}+M,

where
\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu},M:L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))\to L^{2}(I,H^{-1}(\nu))
are given by

(5.8)

\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu}:=\beta\nabla^{2}\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}+\beta(\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid x)}[\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,\cdot)])\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}+\beta A\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}-\beta B(D+\beta^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}C+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{n},

(5.9)

M:=\beta(\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid x)}[\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,\cdot)])\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}-\beta A\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}-\beta\mathrm{Cov}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid x)}[\nabla\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,\cdot),\nabla\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,\cdot)]\\
+\beta B(D+\beta^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}C.

One can prove that the operator
\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu}
has a spectral gap, resulting in the uniqueness of the solution
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
of

\begin{cases}\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu}\psi=v&\text{on $\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})$}\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on $\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})$},\end{cases}

for any
v\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
. We work with general measures
\nu
on
\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})
in order to make our argument flexible enough to be applied to the interpolating measure (
6.7
) of Section
6
.

Assumptions 5.1
.

Let
\nu
be a probability measure on
\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})
in the form
\mathrm{d}\nu=e^{-\beta H^{\mathrm{g}}(x)}\mathrm{d}x
with
H^{\mathrm{g}}:\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\to\mathbb{R}

\mathcal{C}^{2}
and such that

\lim_{d(x,\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}))\to 0}\nabla H^{\mathrm{g}}(x)\cdot\vec{n}=-\infty.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the good event (
5.6
). Assume that there exist
C>0,\delta>0
(depending on
\varepsilon
) such that

\nu(\mathcal{A}^{c})\leq Ce^{-cn^{\delta}}.

For the bootstrap argument to come in Subsection
5.5
, one shall also work with a slightly more general system. Let
A_{0}:\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\to\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
be a measurable map. Let
\mathsf{M}:\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\to\mathcal{S}_{\bar{n}}(\mathbb{R})
be given by

(5.10)

\mathsf{M}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{0}&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix},

with
B
,
C
,
D
constants matrices as in (
5.2
). One shall impose the following assumptions on
\mathsf{M}
:

Assumptions 5.2
.

Let
\mathsf{M}:\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\to\mathcal{S}_{\bar{n}}(\mathbb{R})
be given by (
5.10
). Assume that

(1)

There exists
\kappa>0
such that uniformly on
\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})
,

(5.11)

\mathsf{M}\geq n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}I_{\bar{n}}.

(2)

There exists a family of functions
(\alpha_{i,k})
such that for all
U_{\bar{n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}
,

U_{\bar{n}}\cdot\mathsf{M}U_{\bar{n}}=\sum_{i,k}\alpha_{i,k}(u_{i}+\ldots+u_{k})^{2}.

Moreover there exists
\kappa>0
and
\gamma>0
such that for each
i,k\in\bar{I}
such that
d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\geq n^{\gamma\varepsilon}
,

(5.12)

\frac{n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s+2}}\leq\alpha_{i,k}\leq\frac{n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s+2}}.

(3)

Let

A\in\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
be as in (
5.2
). There exist
C>0,\kappa>0
such that uniformly on (
5.6
) and for each
1\leq i,j\leq n
,

(5.13)

|(A_{0})_{i,j}-A_{i,j}|\leq\frac{Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)^{1+\frac{s}{2}}}.

Finally let
\bar{A}_{1}:L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))\to L^{2}(I,H^{-1}(\nu))
in the form

(5.14)

\bar{A}_{1}=\beta A_{0}-B(\beta D+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}C+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{n}.

Remark 5.3
.

Note that the choice
\bar{n}=\lfloor n^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{s}}\rfloor
when
n\leq\frac{N}{2}
ensures that (
5.13
) holds: indeed there exists
C>0
such that for each
k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
,

|g_{N,s}(k)-g_{\bar{n},s}(k)|\leq\frac{C}{\bar{n}^{s}}\leq\frac{C}{n^{1+\frac{s}{2}}}.

Lemma 5.1
.

(1)

Let
K_{0}=\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor^{m}
. Let
\mathcal{A}
be as in (
5.6
). Set

(5.15)

A_{0}:x\in\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\mapsto\beta\nabla^{2}F^{\mathrm{g}}(x)+\beta(\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}(\cdot\mid x)}[\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,\cdot)])\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(x)+A\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}(x).

Then, for
m
large enough, the
\mathcal{S}_{\bar{n}}(\mathbb{R})
-valued function
\mathsf{M}
(
5.10
) satisfies Assumptions
5.2
.

(2)

Let
\mathsf{M}
in the form (
5.10
) satisfying Assumptions
5.2
. Let
\bar{A}_{1}
be given by (
5.14
). Let
\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

(5.16)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}\psi+(\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}})\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Let
\psi^{(1)}\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

(5.17)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\bar{A}_{1}\psi^{(1)}=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi^{(1)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

We have the identity

(5.18)

\psi_{j}=\psi_{j}^{(1)}\quad\text{for each $j\in I$}.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{M}
be given by (
5.10
) with
A_{0}
as in (
5.15
). Note that by definition of the good event (
5.6
),
\mathsf{M}
verifies (
5.13
). Let us prove that
\mathsf{M}
satisfies (
5.11
). One can split
\mathsf{M}
into
\mathsf{M}^{(1)}+\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
with
\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
given for all
U_{\bar{n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}
by

(5.19)

U_{\bar{n}}\cdot\mathsf{M}^{(2)}U_{\bar{n}}=\sum_{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\geq K_{0}}\alpha_{i,k}(u_{i}+\ldots+u_{k})^{2},

where the
\alpha_{i,k}
’s are as in (
5.12
). Let
\mathrm{H}
be as in (
5.2
) and set
\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}=\mathsf{M}^{(2)}-\mathrm{H}
. There exist
C>0,\kappa>0
such that for each
1\leq i,j\leq n
,

|\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}_{i,j}|\leq\frac{Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)^{1+\frac{s}{2}}}\mathds{1}_{d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\geq K_{0}},

with
\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}_{i,j}=0
if
i\in\bar{I}\setminus I
or
j\in\bar{I}\setminus I
. Let us add to
\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}
the matrix
n^{-\kappa_{1}\varepsilon}I_{\bar{n}}
for some well-chosen constant
\kappa_{1}
. Set
B=n^{-\kappa_{1}\varepsilon}I_{n}+(\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}_{i,j})_{i,j\in I}\in\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
. For
\kappa_{1}
large enough, the matrix
B
is diagonally dominant and its spectrum is thus bounded, according to the Gershgorin circle theorem, by
2n^{-\kappa_{1}\varepsilon}
. Since
B
is symmetric, one gets an upper bound on its Euclidian operator norm, allowing one to bound from above the Euclidian operator norm of
\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}
itself. It follows that there exist
\kappa>0
and
c>0
such that for all
U_{\bar{n}}=(U_{n},V_{\bar{n}-n})\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}
,

\Bigr|U_{\bar{n}}\cdot(\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}-\mathrm{H})U_{\bar{n}}\Bigr|\leq n^{\kappa\varepsilon}K_{0}^{-c\frac{s}{2}}|U_{n}|^{2}.

Besides note that for all
U_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

U_{n}\cdot\mathsf{M}^{(1)}U_{n}\geq n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}|U_{n}|^{2}.

Choosing
K_{0}
large enough one gets the existence of some
\kappa>0,\kappa^{\prime}>0
such that for all
U_{\bar{n}}=(U_{n},V_{\bar{n}-n})\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}
,

U_{\bar{n}}\cdot\mathsf{M}U_{\bar{n}}\geq U_{\bar{n}}\cdot\mathrm{H}U_{\bar{n}}+n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}|U_{n}|^{2}\geq n^{-\kappa^{\prime}\varepsilon}|U_{\bar{n}}|^{2},

thus showing that
\mathsf{M}
verifies (
5.11
). Moreover by construction on the event, the matrix
\mathsf{M}
is a perturbation of
\mathrm{H}
which satisfies (
5.12
) and so does therefore
\mathsf{M}
. The definition of (
5.12
) also ensures that (
5.13
) holds.

The properties (
5.12
) and (
5.13
) holds by definition of the good event (
5.6
) and since the matrix
\mathrm{H}
verifies (
5.12
).

Let us verify the second item. Let
\mathsf{M}
satisfying Assumptions
5.2
. Since
\mathcal{L}^{\nu}
is non-negative we obtain that for all
v\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[v\cdot(\beta\mathsf{M}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}})v]\geq n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|v|^{2}].

By the Lax-Milgram theorem, uniqueness and existence of solutions of (
5.16
) is then a consequence of the coerciveness of

v\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[v\cdot(\beta\mathsf{M}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})v].

It remains to prove the coerciveness of

v\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[v\cdot\bar{A}_{1}v].

Since
\mathrm{H}^{(2)}\geq 0
, we also have that
A^{(2)}-BD^{-1}C\geq 0
. Then note

(5.20)

A_{0}-BD^{-1}C\geq\mathsf{M}^{(1)}+A^{(2)}-BD^{-1}C-O(n^{\kappa\varepsilon}K_{0}^{-\frac{s}{2}}I_{n})\geq\mathsf{M}^{(1)}-O(n^{\kappa\varepsilon}K_{0}^{-\frac{s}{2}}I_{n})\geq n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}I_{n}.

Let
w\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
. One can observe that

w\cdot B(D+\beta^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}(Cw)=(Cw)\cdot(D+\beta^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}(Cw).

Integrating this over
\nu
and using the fact that
D
is positive shows that for all
w\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
,

(5.21)

0\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w\cdot B(D+\beta^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}(Cw)]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w\cdot BD^{-1}Cw].

Consequently, inserting (
5.20
), we find

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w\cdot\bar{A}_{1}w]\geq n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|w|^{2}].

∎

We have thus defined a simplified operator
\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu}
which can be compared
A_{1}^{\nu}
through the following easy argument. Let
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of (
5.1
) and
\psi^{(1)}\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu}\psi^{(1)}=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi^{(1)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Let
w:=\psi^{(1)}-\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
, which solves

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A_{1}^{\nu}w=M\psi^{(1)}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
w\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}),\end{array}\right.

where
M
is an in (
5.9
). Taking the scalar product of the first line of the last display with
w
and integrating by parts with respect to
\nu
yields

(5.22)

\beta n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|w|^{2}]\leq\beta\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w\cdot\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}w]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w\cdot M\psi^{(1)}].

We will prove in Lemma
5.3
that

|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w_{i}(M{\psi}^{(1)})_{i}]|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w_{i}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{j\in I}\frac{1}{1+d(i,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[({\psi}^{(1)}_{j})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Inserting the last display into (
5.22
) will then give

(5.23)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|w|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}n^{\frac{1-s}{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[({\psi}^{(1)}_{j})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}},

where
d
stands for the usual distance on
I
. Our main task is to establish that
{\psi}^{(1)}_{j}
typically decays in
d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{-(2-s)}
, making the left-hand side of (
5.23
) bounded by
n^{-1/2}
. This will show that
\psi_{j}
is bounded by
d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{-(2-s)}+O(n^{-1/2})
, thus concluding the proof of Theorem
1
by choosing
n
large enough.

Let us finally complete Step 4 and control the operator (
5.9
). Recall that
B^{T}=C
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\nu
satisfying Assumptions
5.1
. Let
s\in(0,1)
. Let
B,C,D
be as in (
5.2
). Recall
I=\{1,\ldots,n\}
and let
d
be the usual distance on
I
. There exist
C(\beta),\kappa>0
such that for all
\eta
,
\phi\in L^{2}(\nu)
and for each
1\leq i,j\leq n
, we have

(5.24)

|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\eta Ce_{j})^{T}(\beta D+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}(\phi Ce_{i})]|\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(i,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\eta^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\phi^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In addition for all
\eta,\phi\in L^{2}(\nu)
and for each
1\leq i,j,l\leq n
, we have

(5.25)

|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\eta C(e_{j}-e_{l}))^{T}(\beta D+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}(\phi Ce_{i})]|\\
\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}d(j,l)}{\min(d(j,\partial I)^{1+\frac{s}{2}},d(l,\partial I)^{1+\frac{s}{2}})}\frac{1}{d(i,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\eta^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\phi^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Note that the term in the left-hand side of (
5.24
) resembles the covariance between
\partial_{i}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}
and
\partial_{j}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}
under a Gaussian measure. This suggests us to proceed like for controlling the variances of
\partial_{i}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}
and
\partial_{j}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}
, which requires controlling the fluctuations of large gaps. One may thus import the method of
[
BEY12
, Lem. 3.16-3.17]
which builds on a block decomposition of large gaps to exploit the convexity of the energy at different scales.

Proof.

First note that since
\beta D+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n}
is a positive operator on
L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
, we have

(5.26)

|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\eta Ce_{j})^{T}(\beta D+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}(\phi Ce_{i})]|\\
\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\eta Ce_{j})\cdot(\beta D+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}(\eta Ce_{j})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\phi Ce_{i})\cdot(\beta D+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}(\phi Ce_{i})]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using the positivity of
\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n}
and
D
, one can write

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\eta Ce_{j})\cdot(\beta D+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}(\eta Ce_{j})]\leq\beta^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\eta Ce_{j})\cdot D^{-1}(\eta Ce_{j})]=\beta^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\eta^{2}](Ce_{j})\cdot D^{-1}(Ce_{j}).

The right-hand side of the last display can be identified with the variance of

(CZ)_{j}
where
Z
is a Gaussian vector
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,D)
. Let
g_{\bar{n},s,K_{0}}
be the Riesz kernel truncated at
K_{0}
, defined by

g_{\bar{n},s,K_{0}}(i)=\sum_{k,l\in I_{i,1}:d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)\geq K_{0}}g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime\prime}(i-k),

where
g^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{n},s}
is as in (
2.13
) and
I_{i,1}
defined by

I_{i,1}=\Bigr\{k\in\bar{I}:d_{\bar{n}}(k,\frac{i+1}{2})>\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,1)\Bigr\}.

Recall that for each
j\in I
,
(CZ)_{j}
is given by

(CZ)_{j}=\sum_{k\in\bar{I}\setminus I}g_{\bar{n},s,K_{0}}(j-k)Z_{k}.

Define the random vector
(Y_{k})_{k\in\bar{I}\setminus I}
such that
Y_{n}=0
and for each
k=n+1,\ldots,\bar{n}
,
Z_{k}=Y_{k}-Y_{k-1}
. By discrete integration by parts, one may write

(5.27)

\sum_{k=n+1}^{\lfloor\frac{\bar{n}}{2}\rfloor}g_{\bar{n},s,K_{0}}(j-k)Z_{k}=\sum_{k=n+1}^{\lfloor\frac{\bar{n}}{2}\rfloor}g_{\bar{n},s,K_{0}}(j-k)(Y_{k}-Y_{k-1})\\
=-\sum_{k=n+1}^{\lfloor\frac{\bar{n}}{2}\rfloor}(g_{\bar{n},s,K_{0}}(j-k)-g_{\bar{n},s,K_{0}}(j-k+1))(Y_{k}-Y_{n})+g_{\bar{n},s,K_{0}}(j-\lfloor\frac{\bar{n}}{2}\rfloor)(Y_{\lfloor\frac{\bar{n}}{2}\rfloor}-Y_{n}).

We claim that there exists
C>0
and
\kappa>0
such that for each
n\leq i\leq\bar{n}
and
1\leq i+k\leq\bar{n}/2
,

(5.28)

\mathrm{Var}[Y_{i+k}-Y_{i}]\leq Ck^{s+\kappa\varepsilon}.

Proceeding as in (
5.27
) for the sum between
\lfloor\frac{\bar{n}}{2}\rfloor+1
and
n
entails, modulo (
5.28
),

|e_{j}\cdot BD^{-1}Ce_{j})|\leq\frac{C}{1+d(j,\partial I)^{s/2}}.

Let us now prove the claim (
5.28
). Fix
i,k
such that
n+1\leq i\leq i+k\leq\bar{n}
. Following the lines of
[
BEY12
, Lem. 3.16-3.17]
, one shall split
Y_{i+k}-Y_{i}
into a sum of block averaged statistics. For each
1\leq l\leq n/2
and
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, let
I_{l}(j)
be an interval of integers in
\{n+1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
of cardinal
l+1
such that
j\in I_{l}(j)
. Define the block average

Y_{j}^{[l]}=\frac{1}{l+1}\sum_{m\in I_{l}(j)}Y_{m}.

Let
\alpha=\frac{1}{p}
for a large
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. One may write

(5.29)

Y_{i}-Y_{i}^{[k]}=\sum_{m=0}^{p-1}(Y_{i}^{[\lfloor k^{m\alpha}\rfloor]}-Y_{i}^{[\lfloor k^{(m+1)\alpha}\rfloor]}).

For each
m\in\{0,\ldots,p-1\}
, denote
G_{m}=Y_{i}^{[\lfloor k^{m\alpha}\rfloor]}-Y_{i}^{[\lfloor k^{(m+1)\alpha}\rfloor]}
and
I_{m}=I_{\lfloor k^{(m+1)\alpha}\rfloor}(i)
. Denote
\tilde{H}\in\mathcal{M}_{\bar{n}-n+1}(\mathbb{R})
, the Hessian of the energy (i.e the opposite of the log density) associated to the Gaussian vector
Z
. Note that for each
n+1\leq i,j\leq\bar{n}

\tilde{H}_{i,j}=\begin{cases}g^{\prime\prime}_{s,\bar{n}}(j-i)\mathds{1}_{d(i,j)\geq K_{0}}&\text{if $i\neq j$}\\
-\sum_{k\neq i\in\{n+1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}:d(i,k)\geq K_{1}}g^{\prime\prime}_{s,\bar{n}}(k-i)&\text{if $i=j$}.\end{cases}

On
\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}-n+1}
let us introduce the coordinates

z=((z_{i})_{i\in I_{m}},(z_{i})_{i\in\{n,\ldots,\bar{n}\}\setminus I_{m}}).

Then let
u=(\partial_{i}G_{m})\in\mathbb{R}^{|I_{m}|}
. Let us decompose on
\mathbb{R}^{|I_{m}|}\times\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}-n+1-|I_{m}|}
into

\tilde{H}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{A}&\tilde{B}\\
\tilde{C}&\tilde{D}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{A}\in\mathcal{M}_{|I_{m}|}(\mathbb{R}).

Using the Schur complement formula, one may express the variance of
G_{m}
as

\mathrm{Var}[G_{m}]=u\cdot(\tilde{A}-\tilde{B}\tilde{D}^{-1}\tilde{C})^{-1}u.

Now define the matrix
A\in\mathcal{M}_{|I_{m}|}(\mathbb{R})
given for each
1\leq i,j\leq|I_{m}|
by

\tilde{A}^{(1)}_{i,j}=\begin{cases}g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime\prime}(j-i)\mathds{1}_{d(i,j)\geq K_{0}}&\text{if $i\neq j$}\\
-\sum_{k\neq i\in I_{m}:d(i,k)\geq K_{0}}g^{\prime\prime}_{s,\bar{n}}(k-i)&\text{if $i=j$}.\end{cases}

The point is that
\tilde{A}-\tilde{B}\tilde{D}^{-1}\tilde{C}=\tilde{A}^{(1)}+\tilde{A}^{(2)}-\tilde{B}\tilde{D}^{-1}\tilde{C}\geq\tilde{A}^{(1)}
, since
\tilde{A}^{(2)}-\tilde{B}\tilde{D}^{-1}\tilde{C}
is the Schur complement of a positive-definite matrix. It thus follows that

\mathrm{Var}[G_{m}]\leq u\cdot(\tilde{A}^{(1)})^{-1}u.

Let
v=(\tilde{A}^{(1)})^{-1}u
. Using the fact that
\sum_{i\in I_{m}}\partial_{i}G_{m}=0
and
\tilde{A}^{(1)}\sum_{i\in I_{m}}e_{i}=0
, one may check that
\sum_{i\in I_{m}}v_{i}=0
. It follows that

v\cdot\tilde{A}^{(1)}v\geq\sum_{i\neq j\in I_{m}:d(i,j)\geq K_{0}}g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime\prime}(i-j)(v_{i}-v_{j})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{|I_{m}|^{s+1}}|v|^{2}.

Furthermore observe that

|\nabla G_{m}|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{|I_{m}|}.

By integration by parts, the two last displays give

\beta\frac{1}{|I_{m}|^{s+1}}|v|^{2}\leq v\cdot\tilde{A}^{(1)}v\leq C|v|\frac{1}{|I_{m}|}.

It follows that
|v|\leq C|I_{m}|^{\frac{1}{2}+s}
which entails

(5.30)

\mathrm{Var}[G_{m}]\leq C|I_{m}|^{s}.

Summing (
5.30
) over
m
and using (
5.29
), one finds that

\mathrm{Var}[Y_{i+k}-Y_{i}]\leq Ck^{s+\kappa\varepsilon},

which yields (
5.28
), thus concluding the proof of (
5.24
).

The proof of (
5.25
) follows from the estimate (
5.28
) and the inequality

(5.31)

|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\eta C(e_{j}-e_{l}))^{T}(\beta D+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}-n})^{-1}(\phi Ce_{i})]|\\
\leq\beta^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\eta^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\phi^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}(C(e_{j}-e_{l})\cdot D^{-1}C(e_{j}-e_{l}))^{\frac{1}{2}}(Ce_{i}\cdot D^{-1}Ce_{i})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

∎

Let us now control the operator
M
appearing in (
5.9
).

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the good event (
5.6
). Let
d
be the usual distance on
\{1,\ldots,n\}
. There exist
\kappa>0,C(\beta)>0
such that uniformly in
x\in\mathcal{A}
,
1\leq i\leq j\leq n
and
N
, we have

(5.32)

\mathrm{Var}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid x)}[\partial_{i}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}},\partial_{j}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}]\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(i,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}.

Let
\nu
satisfying Assumptions
4.1
and
M
be as in (
5.9
). There exist
\kappa>0,C(\beta)>0
such that for all
\phi,\eta\in L^{2}(\nu)
and each
1\leq i,j\leq n
,

(5.33)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\phi e_{i}\cdot M(\eta e_{j})]\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(i,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\eta^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\phi^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

and for each
1\leq i,l,j\leq n
,

(5.34)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\phi e_{i})\cdot M(\eta(e_{j}-e_{l}))]\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\frac{d(i,l)}{(d(i,\partial I)\wedge d(l,\partial I))^{1+\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\phi^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\eta^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
+C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\sup|\phi|\sup|\eta|.

Proof.

The control (
5.32
) is a direct consequence a rigidity estimate under
\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}(\cdot\mid x)
that we defer to Lemma
6.2
in the Appendix. Regarding the definition of (
5.9
), the bound on the Schur complement (
5.33
) follows from (
5.32
) and Lemma
5.2
. Since
\mathcal{A}
has overwhelming probability one may bound the contribution involving
(\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid x)}[\nabla^{2}\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,\cdot)])\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}-A\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}
by
C(\beta)\sup|\phi|\sup|\eta|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}
.
∎

5.2.
Elliptic regularity estimate

The aim is now to prove a decay estimate on the solution of (
5.17
). We first derive an elliptic regularity estimate and give an
L^{2}
bound on the discrete primitive of order
\frac{3}{2}-s
of
\psi
in terms of
|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|
. We then state a straightforward control on the
L^{1}
norm of the discrete primitive of order
1-s
of
\psi
with respect to
|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|
. By interpolation, this yields via a discrete Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality a control on the
L^{p}
norm of the fractional primitive of order
1-\frac{s}{2}
of
\psi
for
p=\frac{1}{1-s/2}
. Throughout the section, for all
\alpha>0
,
\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}
stands for the distortion matrix

(5.35)

\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}=\mathrm{diag}(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{\bar{n}})\quad\text{with}\quad\gamma_{i}=1+d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}\quad\text{for each $1\leq i\leq\bar{n}$}.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
s\in(0,1)
. Let
\nu
and
\mathsf{M}
satisfying Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
. Let
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}(\nu)
,
i_{0}\in\bar{I}
and
\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

(5.36)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}\psi+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})=0&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Recalling (
5.35
), there exists
\kappa>0
such that letting
p=\frac{1}{1-s/2}
,

(5.37)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\Bigr(\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}|(g_{\bar{n},s/2}*\psi)_{i}|^{p}\Bigr)^{2/p}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\chi_{n}|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+n\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr)^{s}\\
\times\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1-s}{2}}.

Proof.

Let us denote
v=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})
. Let
\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of (
5.36
). In view of (
5.13
), the matrix
\mathsf{M}
may be split into
\mathsf{M}=\mathsf{M}^{(1)}+\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
where
\mathsf{M}^{(1)}\in\mathcal{M}_{\bar{n}}(\mathbb{R})
is the constant Toeplitz matrix with the Riesz kernel
g_{\bar{n},s}
and
\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
satisfying

|\mathsf{M}^{(2)}_{i,j}|\leq\frac{n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)^{1+\frac{s}{2}}},\quad\text{for each $i,j\in\bar{I}$}.

Taking the convolution of (
5.36
) with
g_{\bar{n},s-1}
and the scalar product with
\psi
easily gives

(5.38)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}(g_{\bar{n},s-1/2}*\psi)_{i}^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\chi_{n}|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+n\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr).

Indeed, the differential terms satisfies

\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathcal{L}^{\nu}((g_{\bar{n},s-1}*\psi)_{i})\psi_{i}]=\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\nabla(g_{\bar{n},s-1}*\psi)_{i}\cdot\nabla\psi_{i}]=\sum_{i,j,k}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[g_{\bar{n},s-1}(k-i)\partial_{j}\psi_{k}\cdot\partial_{j}\psi_{i}].

Since
g_{\bar{n},s-1}
is a positive kernel, for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
, setting
u_{k}=\partial_{j}\psi_{k}
, we have

\sum_{i,k}g_{\bar{n},s-1}(k-i)u_{i}u_{k}\geq 0,

which justifies the claim (
5.38
).

Recall that by Remark
2.1
, the convolution of a discrete function
f:\mathbb{Z}/\bar{n}\mathbb{Z}
with
g_{\bar{n},\alpha}
for
\alpha>-1
corresponds to a fractional primitive of order
1-\alpha
of
f
. One can now interpolate between the
L^{1}
norm of the primitive of
\psi
of order
1-s
and the
L^{2}
norm of the primitive of order
1-\frac{s}{2}
. Let
\phi:\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{R}
smooth enough. Applying Lemma
A.1
to
u:=g_{\bar{n},s-1/2}*\psi
with
s_{1}=0
,
s_{2}=\frac{1}{2}
,
s_{0}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}\in(s_{1},s_{2})
,
\theta=s
,
p_{1}=2
,
p_{2}=1
and
p=\frac{1}{1-\frac{s}{2}}
gives

(5.39)

\|g_{\bar{n},s/2}*\psi\|_{L^{\frac{1}{1-s/2}}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\|g_{\bar{n},s}*\psi\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T})}^{\theta}\|g_{\bar{n},s-1/2}*\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{1-\theta}.

Let
\phi_{0}:\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{R}
smooth such that
\phi_{0}(\frac{i}{\bar{n}})=\psi_{i}
for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
. Using (
5.39
) and making
\phi_{0}
slightly vary, we deduce that

(5.40)

\Bigr(\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}|(g_{\bar{n},s/2}*\psi)_{i}|^{\frac{1}{1-s/2}}\Bigr)^{1-s/2}\leq C\Bigr(\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}|(g_{\bar{n},s}*\psi)_{i}|\Bigr)^{1-s}\Bigr(\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}(g_{\bar{n},s-1/2}*\psi)_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{s}{2}}.

Besides, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it is straightforward to check that

(5.41)

\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}|(g_{\bar{n},s}*\psi)_{i}|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|.

Inserting (
5.41
) and (
5.38
) into (
5.40
), one obtains (
5.37
).
∎

5.3.
Control on derivatives

The aim is now to control the global decay of
\nabla\psi_{i}
with respect to the global decay of
\psi_{i}
. The proof relies on the distortion argument of Lemma
4.2
, the central task being to bound a variant of the commutator
\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{M}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}-\mathsf{M}
from above.

Let us pause to explain the strategy of this proof. At first let us fix a small parameter
\varepsilon_{0}>0
. In view of its specific positive-definiteness structure,
\mathsf{M}
can be bounded from below by a matrix
\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}
where interactions are cut off for
d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)>d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}
. We then seek to control
(\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{M}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}-\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}})_{i}
for each
1\leq i\leq\bar{n}
. By construction,
(\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}})_{i}
may be bounded by
|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s-\varepsilon_{0}}\psi|
. Similarly one can bound the left and right tails of
(\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{M}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}})_{i}
by
|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s-\varepsilon_{0}}\psi|
. We are thus left to estimate

(5.42)

\sum_{k\in A(i)}\psi_{k}{g}_{\bar{n},s}(k-i)\quad\text{where}\quad A(i):=\{k\neq i:d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\varepsilon_{0}}\}.

The point is to express this sum with respect
w:=\mathbb{H}_{\bar{n},s/2}\psi
, the discrete primitive
w
of order
1-s/2
of
\psi
, which gives

(5.43)

\sum_{k\in A(i)}\psi_{k}{g}_{\bar{n},s}(d_{\bar{n}}(i,k))=\sum_{l=1}^{\bar{n}}\sum_{k\in A(i)}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)){g}_{\bar{n},s}(d_{\bar{n}}(i,k))\mathds{1}_{i\neq k}w_{l},

where
g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}:=\mathbb{H}_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}e_{1}
. Given an index
l
, one shall therefore estimate a
truncated
convolution product between
g_{\bar{n},s}
and
g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}
. If
l
lies away from the boundary of
A(i)
, this product almost equals
g_{\bar{n},s}*g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(l)\simeq g_{\bar{n},1-s/2}(l)
. Fixing a threshold of size
d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}
, one can decompose (
5.43
) according to whether
d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))\geq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}
. Owing to the previous remark and by Hölder’s inequality, one can bound the first contribution by the
L^{p}
norm of
w
with
p=\frac{1}{1-s/2}
and insert (
5.37
). On the other hand, the second contribution can be controlled by
|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s-\varepsilon_{0}}\psi|
.

We finally obtain a control on
|\mathrm{L}_{1-s/2}D\psi|
depending on
|\mathrm{L}_{1-s/2-\varepsilon_{0}}\psi|
and on
n^{\varepsilon_{0}}|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|
. A reversed inequality will be proved in the next subsection allowing one to control
|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|
by
|\mathrm{L}_{1-s/2}\psi|
. Since
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
3/2-s>1/2
, this will provide a bound on
|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|
and
|\mathrm{L}_{1-s/2}D\psi|
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
s\in(0,1)
. Let
\nu
and
\mathsf{M}
satisfying Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
. Let
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}(\nu)
,
i_{0}\in\bar{I}
and
\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

(5.44)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}\psi+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})=0&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Let
\alpha_{0}\in(\frac{1-2s}{1-s},1)
as in Lemma
5.4
. Let
\gamma\geq\frac{1}{2}
. There exist
C(\beta)
locally uniform in
\beta
,
\kappa>0
,
\delta>0
and
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that

(5.45)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d(i,i_{0})^{2(\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{4})}|\nabla\psi_{i}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{\gamma}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr(n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1-\alpha_{0}}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}\\
+n^{-\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}+1}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr)+n^{\kappa(\varepsilon_{0}+\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}].

Proof.

Let
\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of (
5.44
).

Step 1: a priori estimates and distortion

First note that
\psi
satisfies the energetic estimate

(5.46)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|D\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For
\alpha\geq\frac{1}{2}
, let
\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{M}_{\bar{n}}(\mathbb{R})
be as in (
5.35
). Let
\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}=\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\psi
. Multiplying (
5.65
) by
\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}
, one can see that
\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}
solves

\beta\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{M}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}}).

In contrast with the short-range case, one cannot expect
|\mathsf{M}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}|
to be of order
n^{\kappa\varepsilon}
under
\nu
if
\alpha=\frac{3}{2}-s
and one should therefore not split
\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{M}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}
into
\mathsf{M}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}+(\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{M}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}-\mathsf{M})\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}
. We will instead isolate short-range interactions. Fix a small parameter
\varepsilon_{0}>0
. For each
i,j\in\bar{I}
, let

I_{i,j}:=\Bigr\{k\in\bar{I}:d_{\bar{n}}(k,\frac{i+j}{2})>\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\Bigr\}.

By Assumptions
4.1
item (
5.12
), there exists a family of functions
(\alpha_{i,j})_{i,j\in\bar{I}}
satisfying for
\alpha_{i,j}\geq 0
for each
d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\geq n^{\varepsilon\gamma}
such that

U_{N}\cdot\mathsf{M}U_{N}=\sum_{k\neq l}\alpha_{k,l}(u_{k}+\ldots+u_{l})^{2}\geq\sum_{k\neq l:d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\alpha_{k,l}(u_{k}+\ldots+u_{l})^{2}:=U_{N}\cdot\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}U_{N}.

It follows that
\mathsf{M}\geq\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}
, where

\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}_{i,j}:=\sum_{k\neq l\in I_{i,j}:d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\alpha_{k,l}.

Let
l_{0}:=\lfloor d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}\rfloor
. Let also
\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}^{(1)}
defined for each
i,j\in\bar{I}
by

(5.47)

\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}_{i,j}^{(1)}=\begin{cases}{g}_{\bar{n},s}(j-i)-{g}_{\bar{n},s}(l_{0})-g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime}(l_{0})(d_{\bar{n}}(j,i)-l_{0})&\text{if $d_{\bar{n}}(j,i)\leq l_{0}$}\\
0&\text{if $d_{\bar{n}}(j,i)>l_{0}$}.\end{cases}

Finally let
\mathsf{M}^{(2)}=\mathsf{M}-\mathbb{H}_{\bar{n},s}
be the random part of
\mathsf{M}
and set

\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(1)}=\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathbb{H}_{\bar{n},s}\mathrm{L}^{-1}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}^{(1)}\quad\text{and}\quad\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(2)}=\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{M}^{(2)}\mathrm{L}^{-1}_{\alpha}-{\mathsf{M}}^{(2)},

so that
\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}
is solution of

(5.48)

\beta\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}+\beta\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(1)}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}+\beta\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(2)}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}}).

Step 2: integration by parts

We proceed as in the proof of Lemma
4.2
. Taking the scalar product of (
5.48
) with
\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}
reads

(5.49)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\beta\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot(\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}+\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(1)}+\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(2)})\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}]+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi_{i_{0}}\chi_{n}+\mathrm{L}_{2\alpha}\psi\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})\lambda].

By construction, there exists a constant
\kappa_{0}>0
such that

(5.50)

\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}\geq n^{-\kappa_{0}\varepsilon}I_{\bar{n}}.

It therefore remains to control the commutators
\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(1)}
and
\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(2)}
.

Step 3: control on the long-range commutator

This step is the most important of the proof. Recalling that
\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathbb{H}_{\bar{n},s}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}=\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathbb{H}_{\bar{n},s}\psi
, one may split
\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(1)}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}
into

(5.51)

(\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(1)}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}})_{i}=\underbrace{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\geq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\hskip-14.22636ptg_{\bar{n},s}(i-k)\psi_{k}}_{({I})_{i}}\\
+\underbrace{\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\hskip-14.22636pt{g}_{\bar{n},s}(i-k)\Bigr(\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{\alpha}}-1\Bigr)\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}}_{({II})_{i}}+({III})_{i},

with

({III})_{i}=g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime}(d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}})\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}(d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)-d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}})\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}\\
-{g}_{\bar{n},s}(d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}})\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}.

Let us split
({I})_{i}
further into

({I})_{i}=\underbrace{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\varepsilon_{0}}}{g}_{\bar{n},s}(i-k)\psi_{k}}_{({I})^{\prime}_{i}}+\underbrace{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)>d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\varepsilon_{0}}}{g}_{\bar{n},s}(i-k)\psi_{k}}_{({I})^{\prime\prime}_{i}}.

First note that by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

|({I})^{\prime\prime}_{i}|\leq C\Bigr(\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)>d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{2s}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i_{0},k)^{3-2s}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|\leq\frac{C}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\varepsilon_{0}}}|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|.

We turn to the term
({I})^{\prime}_{i}
. The idea is to express it with respect to the primitive of order
1-s/2
of
\psi
and to use the
L^{\frac{1}{1-s/2}}
control of Lemma
5.4
. Let
w=\mathbb{H}_{\bar{n},s/2}\psi
and
g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}=\mathbb{H}_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}e_{1}
. One may write

(5.52)

({I})_{i}^{\prime}=\sum_{l=1}^{\bar{n}}\Bigr(\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k-l)\Bigr)w_{l}.

The value of the truncated convolution product in front of
w_{l}
depends on whether
l
lies close to the boundary of
A(i):=\{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\varepsilon_{0}}\}
. We claim that there exists a constant
C>0
such that for each
l\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
,

(5.53)

\Bigr|\sum_{k\in A(i)}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k-l)\Bigr|\leq C\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{s}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))^{1-s/2}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{1+\frac{s}{2}-\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}}\Bigr).

Let us prove (
5.53
). First, in view of Lemma
2.3
, the kernel
g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}
satisfies

(5.54)

|g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}|(k)\leq\frac{C}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,1)^{2-s/2}}\quad\text{for each $1\leq k\leq\bar{n}$},

(5.55)

\Bigr|\sum_{k=1}^{\bar{n}}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k)\Bigr|\leq\frac{C}{n^{1-\frac{s}{2}}}.

If
d_{\bar{n}}(l,A(i))\geq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})
, then by (
5.54
), the result if straightforward. Now if
l\in A(i)
with
d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))\geq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})
, one can write

\sum_{k\in A(i)}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k-l)=-\sum_{k\in A(i)}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k-l)=O\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\frac{s}{2}-\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}}\Bigr).

Finally let
l
such that
d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})
. One has

\sum_{k\in A(i)}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k-l)=\sum_{k\in A(i):d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)\leq\frac{3}{4}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k-l)\\
+\sum_{k\in A(i):d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)>\frac{3}{4}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k-l).

In view of (
5.54
) there holds

\Bigr|\sum_{k\in A(i):d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)>\frac{3}{4}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k-l)\Bigr|\leq\frac{C}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\frac{s}{2}-\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}}.

Let us split the first term by writing

\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}=\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{s}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}-\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{s}}.

Since
d_{\bar{n}}(l,A(i))\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})
and
d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)\leq\frac{3}{4}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})
one has

(5.56)

\Bigr|\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}-\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{s}}\Bigr|\leq\frac{Cd_{\bar{n}}(k,l)}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+s}}.

Using in turn (
5.54
) and (
5.55
), one can see that

\sum_{k\in A(i):d_{\bar{n}}(k,i)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k-l)=\sum_{k\in A(i)}g_{\bar{n},s/2}^{-1}(k-l)+O\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-s/2}}\Bigr)\\
=O\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))^{1-s/2}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-s/2}}\Bigr).

Finally inserting (
5.56
) we have

\Bigr|\sum_{k\in A(i):d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}-\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{s}}\Bigr)\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)^{2-{s}/{2}}}\Bigr|\leq C\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{s/2}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{1+s}}\leq\frac{C}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{1+\frac{s}{2}-\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}}.

Combining the two last displays, one obtains the claimed estimate (
5.53
).

Let us split the sum over
l
in (
5.52
) according to whether
d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))\geq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}
. For the first contribution one can write

\Bigr|\sum_{l:d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))\geq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{s}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))^{1-s/2}}w_{l}\Bigr|\leq Cd_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\sum_{l:d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)\geq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{1+\frac{s}{2}}}|w_{l}|\\
\leq Cd_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\Bigr(\sum_{l=1}^{\bar{n}}|w_{l}|^{\frac{1}{1-s/2}}\Bigr)^{1-s/2}\Bigr(\sum_{l:d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)\geq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{\frac{2}{s}(1+\frac{s}{2})}}\Bigr)^{\frac{s}{2}}\\
\leq\frac{C}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}}\Bigr(\sum_{l=1}^{\bar{n}}|w_{l}|^{\frac{1}{1-s/2}}\Bigr)^{1-s/2}.

Inserting the estimate (
5.37
) of Lemma
5.4
then yields

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\Bigr|\sum_{l:d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))\geq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{s}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))^{1-s/2}}w_{l}\Bigr|^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}})^{s}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1-s}{2}}.

For the second contribution, one can check via Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

|w_{l}|\leq\frac{C}{d_{\bar{n}}(l,i_{0})^{1-s/2}}|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|.

It follows that

(5.57)

\Bigr|\sum_{l:d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,l)^{s}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))^{1-\frac{s}{2}}}w_{l}\Bigr|\\
\leq C\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\frac{s}{2}}}\sum_{l:d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial A(i))^{1-\frac{s}{2}}}|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|\leq\frac{C}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+s\varepsilon_{0}}}|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|.

We have crucially used the fact that in (
5.57
), the series
\sum_{k}\frac{1}{k^{1-{s}/{2}}}
is diverging, in order to have an error in the last display much smaller than
d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{-1}
, when
\varepsilon_{0}>0
. This justifies our choice of considering a fractional primitive of order
1-s/2
(rather than
3/2-s
for instance). One can gather these estimates into

(5.58)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[({I})_{i}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\alpha}}\Bigr(\Bigr(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\chi_{n}|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}+n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr)^{s}\\
+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1-s}{2}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+s\varepsilon_{0}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr).

We now control the terms
({II})_{i}
and
({III})_{i}
. Let us write
({II})_{i}
as

({II})_{i}=\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}(d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}-d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{\alpha})\psi_{k}\\
=d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{s}}\Bigr(1-\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{\alpha}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}}\Bigr)\psi_{k}.

One can Taylor expand the weight in the above equation when
d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}
into

\Bigr|1-\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{\alpha}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}}\Bigr|\leq C\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}.

This allows one to bound
({II})_{i}
by

(5.59)

|({II})_{i}|\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha-1}\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{1-s}|\psi_{k}|\leq\frac{C}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\alpha+(1-s)\varepsilon_{0}}}|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|.

Similarly, by expanding
d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{\alpha}
for
k
close to
i
, one obtains

(5.60)

|({III})_{i}|\leq\frac{C}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1-\alpha+\varepsilon_{0}(1-s)}}|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|.

Putting (
5.58
), (
5.59
) and (
5.60
) together, one obtains that for
\varepsilon_{0}>0
large enough with respect to
\varepsilon
, there exists
\kappa>0
such that

(5.61)

|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(1)}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}]|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{2\alpha-1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr(n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+n^{-\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{s}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1-s}{2}}+n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr)+C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}].

Step 4: control on the short-range commutator

It remains to bound
\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(2)}
. Recall that by (
5.10
), the off-diagonal entries of
\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
typically decays in
d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)^{-(1+\frac{s}{2})}
. One may write

(\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(2)}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}})_{i}=\underbrace{\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\mathsf{M}^{(2)}_{i,k}\Bigr(\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,1)^{\alpha}}-1\Bigr)\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}}_{({I})_{i}}+\underbrace{\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)>\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\mathsf{M}^{(2)}_{i,k}\Bigr(\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,1)^{\alpha}}-1\Bigr)\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}}_{({II})_{i}}.

The first term can be bounded for any value of
\alpha
by

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[({I})_{i}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

with
C(\beta)
depending on
\alpha
. For the second term we have

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[({II})_{i}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{1+\frac{s}{2}-\alpha}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Consequently arguing as in the short-range case (see the proof of Lemma
4.2
) we obtain

\Bigr|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}({I})_{i}\Bigr]\Bigr|\leq\frac{\beta}{2}n^{-\varepsilon(s+2)}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}]+C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}]\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By construction, we have

(5.62)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}+\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}({I})_{i}]\Bigr]\geq 0.

For the second term, the point is to give a control in term of
\mathrm{L}_{2\alpha-1/2}\psi
:

(5.63)

\Bigr|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}({II})_{i}\Bigr]\Bigr|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{2\alpha-1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Step 5: conclusion

Note that for
\alpha\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
2\alpha-\frac{1}{2}\geq\alpha
. Therefore in view of (
5.61
), (
5.62
) and (
5.63
) we obtain from (
5.49
) that for
\alpha\geq\frac{1}{2}
,

(5.64)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2\alpha}|\nabla\psi_{i}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{2\alpha-1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr(n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{s}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1-s}{2}}\\
+n^{-\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}+n\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr)+n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]\Bigr).

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.5
.
∎

5.4.
Global decay estimate

Leveraging on the a priori estimate of Lemma
5.5
, we establish a global decay estimate on the solution. The method uses a factorization of the system around its ground state to reduce the problem to the well-understood short-range situation of Section
4
. Let us emphasize that due to the degeneracy of the inverse of the Riesz matrix (
2.5
), it is unavoidable to have an a priori control on
D\psi
such as (
5.45
).

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
s\in(0,1)
. Let
\nu
and
\mathsf{M}
satisfying Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
. Let
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}(\nu)
,
i_{0}\in\bar{I}
and
\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

(5.65)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}\psi+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})=0&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

There exists a constant
C(\beta)
locally uniform in
\beta
and
\kappa>0
such that

(5.66)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2-s}|\nabla\psi_{i}|^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{3-2s}\psi_{i}^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In addition, there exist a constant
C(\beta)
locally uniform in
\beta
and
\kappa>0
such that

(5.67)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{1-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

The strategy of proof is to multiply the system (
5.65
) by a constant matrix close to the inverse of
\mathbb{H}_{\bar{n},s}
, so that the system becomes short-range. There are two main difficulties: first, the interaction matrix should still be positive-definite and second, one should be able to control the differential terms involving
\nabla\psi
, which do not come as a non-negative contribution anymore.

Step 1: factorization

To solve the first issue, the idea is to define a kernel
f
which is vanishing outside a certain grid centered at
1
and of length
K_{1}=\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor^{\kappa_{0}}
for some
\kappa_{0}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Assume first that
m:=\frac{\bar{n}}{K_{1}}\in\mathbb{N}
. Define
f:\bar{I}\to\mathbb{R}
given for each
k\in\bar{I}
by

(5.68)

f(k)=\begin{cases}g_{m,s}^{-1}(\frac{k}{K_{1}})&\text{if $k\in\bar{I}\cap K_{1}\mathbb{Z}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

where
g_{m,s}^{-1}=\mathbb{H}_{m,s}^{-1}e_{1}
. Also let
\mathsf{A}
be the Toeplitz matrix associated to
f
:

(5.69)

\mathsf{A}:=(f(j-i))_{i,j}\in\mathcal{M}_{\bar{n}}(\mathbb{R}).

Let us first show that
f
is a positive-definite kernel on
\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
. Let
\theta\in\{\frac{2k\pi}{\bar{n}}:0\leq k\leq\bar{n}-1\}
. One may notice that

\sum_{k=0}^{\bar{n}-1}f(k)e^{ik\theta}=\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}g_{m,s}^{-1}(k)e^{ik\theta K_{1}}.

Since
K_{1}\theta\in\{\frac{2k\pi}{m}:0\leq k\leq m-1\}
, the above sum is positive. It follows that (
5.68
) defines a positive-definite kernel and (
5.69
) a positive-definite matrix.
Now assume that
\frac{\bar{n}}{K_{1}}\notin\mathbb{N}
. Let
m=\lfloor\frac{\bar{n}}{K_{1}}\rfloor
and
f:\{1,\ldots,mK_{1}\}\to\mathbb{R}
be as in (
5.68
). We define

\mathsf{A}:=(f(j-i)\mathds{1}_{-i_{0}\leq j-i\leq mK_{1}})_{i,j}.

Decomposing
\mathsf{A}
into blocks and using the uniform positivity of the first block shows that there exists
\kappa>0,C>0
such that for all
U_{\bar{n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}
,

U_{\bar{n}}\cdot\mathsf{A}U_{\bar{n}}\geq n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}|U_{\bar{n}}|^{2}-Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\sum_{k:d(k,i_{0})\leq K_{1}}|u_{k}|^{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}|U_{N}|.

Step 2: positiveness of
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}

Assume that
\frac{\bar{n}}{K_{1}}\in\mathbb{N}
. One argues that for
K_{1}
large enough, the matrix
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}
is positive-definite, which is quite delicate since the product of two positive-definite matrices is not in general positive-definite. The idea is to split
\mathsf{M}
into the sum of a Toeplitz matrix associated to a positive kernel and a random “diagonally dominant” positive matrix. In view of Assumptions
5.2
, there exists a family of
(\alpha_{i,j})_{i,j\in\bar{I}}
satisfying
\alpha_{i,j}\geq 0
for each
i,j\in\bar{I}
with
d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\geq n^{\varepsilon\gamma}
such that

U_{N}\cdot\mathsf{M}U_{N}=\sum_{k\neq l}\alpha_{k,l}(u_{k}+\ldots+u_{l})^{2}.

Split
\mathsf{M}
into
\mathsf{M}=\mathsf{M}^{(1)}+\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
with for each
1\leq i,j\leq\bar{n}
,

\mathsf{M}^{(1)}_{i,j}=\sum_{k\neq l\in I_{i,j}}\alpha_{k,l}\mathds{1}_{d_{\bar{n}}(k,l)\leq K_{1}},

where
I_{i,j}:=\{k\in\bar{I}:d_{\bar{n}}(k,\frac{i+j}{2})>\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\}
. Since
\mathsf{M}_{i,j}^{(1)}=0
if
d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)>K_{1}
observe that
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(1)}=f(1)\mathsf{M}^{(1)}
. Consequently there exists
\kappa_{1}>0
independent of
K_{1}
such that

(5.70)

\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(1)}\geq n^{-\kappa_{1}\varepsilon}I_{\bar{n}}.

To control the product of
\mathsf{A}
with the long-range matrix
\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
, one may split
\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
into a constant Toeplitz matrix and a random part. Let
h
be the Riesz kernel truncated at
K_{1}
defined for each
k\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
by

(5.71)

h(k):=\sum_{i,j\in I_{1,k}}g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime\prime}(j-i)\mathds{1}_{d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\geq K_{1}},

where
g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime\prime}
is as in (
2.13
). By construction,
h
is a non-negative kernel since for all
U_{\bar{n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}
,

\sum_{i,j}h(i-j)u_{i}u_{j}=\sum_{i,j}g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime\prime}(j-i)\mathds{1}_{d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\geq K_{1}}(u_{i}-u_{j})^{2}.

Denote
\mathsf{M}^{(2,1)}
the Toeplitz matrix associated to
h
and
\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}=\mathsf{M}^{(2)}-\mathsf{M}^{(2,1)}
. Since Toeplitz matrices do commute, the product of
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{M}^{(2,1)}
is non-negative. For the random part note that uniformly in
1\leq i,j\leq\bar{n}
,

|(\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)})_{i,j}|\leq\frac{Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)^{1+\frac{s}{2}}}\mathds{1}_{d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\geq K_{1}},

We wish to bound the Euclidian operator norm of
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}
. Although
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}
is not symmetric, one may proceed as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
. Let
B:=n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}I_{\bar{n}}+\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(2,2)}
. For
K_{1}
large enough,
BB^{T}
is diagonally dominant and its off-diagonal terms satisfy

\max_{i}\sum_{k\neq i}|(BB^{T})_{i,k}|\leq Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}K_{1}^{-s/2}.

Therefore from the Gershgorin circle theorem, the spectrum of
BB^{T}
satisfies

\mathrm{Spec}(BB^{T})\subset[n^{-\kappa_{1}\varepsilon}-Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}K_{1}^{-s/2},n^{-\kappa_{1}\varepsilon}+Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}K_{1}^{-s/2}].

The Euclidian operator norm of
B
being its largest singular value, we obtain that there exists
\kappa>0
such that

\sup_{|x|=1}|x\cdot Bx|\leq n^{-\kappa\varepsilon},

which also gives

(5.72)

\sup_{|x|=1}|x\cdot\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(2)}x|\leq Cn^{-\kappa\varepsilon},

for some constant
C>0
. This can be made much smaller than the lower bound in (
5.70
) by choosing
K_{1}
large enough, thus proving that
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
is positive-definite. In conclusion, if
\frac{\bar{n}}{K_{1}}\in\mathbb{N}
with
K_{1}
large enough, there exists
\kappa>0
such that on (
5.6
),

\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}\geq n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}I_{\bar{n}}.

To summarize, on the first hand the positivity of
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(1)}
follows from the grid construction of (
5.68
), the positivity of
\mathsf{M}^{(1)}
and (
5.72
). On the second hand the positivity of
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(2,1)}
follows from the fact
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{M}^{(2,1)}
are positive and commute.

Now, if
\frac{\bar{n}}{K_{1}}\notin\mathbb{N}
, then for all
U_{\bar{n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}}
,

(5.73)

U_{\bar{n}}\cdot\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}U_{\bar{n}}\geq n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}I_{\bar{n}}-Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\sum_{i:d(i,i_{0})\leq K_{1}}|u_{i}|^{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}U_{\bar{n}}|.

We will apply (
5.73
) to
\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}:=\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\psi
and control
\sum_{d(i,i_{0})\leq K_{1}}(\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}})^{2}
by
K_{1}^{2\alpha}|\psi|^{2}
.

Step 3: decay of
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}

Finally, one may show that the kernel (
5.68
) defines a good approximation of
g_{\bar{n},s}^{-1}
: choosing
K_{1}
to be a large power of
\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor
as before, one can check that there exists a constant
\kappa>0
such that for each
k\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
,

(5.74)

|g_{\bar{n},s}*f|(k)\leq\frac{Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{1+d_{\bar{n}}(k,1)^{2-s}}.

Let
i\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}\cap K_{1}\mathbb{Z}
. Using that for each
k\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
g_{m,s}(k)=g_{\bar{n},s}(kK_{0})K_{0}^{s}
, we find

\sum_{k=1}^{\bar{n}}{g}_{\bar{n},s}(k-i)f(k)=K_{1}^{-s}\mathds{1}_{l=1}.

Now if
i\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
, one can write it as
i=i_{0}+(i-i_{0})
with
i_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}\cap K_{1}\mathbb{Z}
and
d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})\leq K_{1}
. Therefore, by Taylor expansion,

\Bigr|\sum_{k=1}^{\bar{n}}{g}_{\bar{n},s}(k-i)f(k)-\sum_{k=1}^{\bar{n}}{g}_{\bar{n},s}(k-i_{0})f(k)-O(K_{1})\sum_{k=1}^{\bar{n}}{g}_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime}(k-i)f(k)\Bigr|\\
\leq CK_{1}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\bar{n}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{2+s}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,1)^{2-s}}\leq\frac{CK_{1}^{2}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,1)^{2-s}},

where
g_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime}
is as in (
2.14
). Also note that

\Bigr|\sum_{k=1}^{\bar{n}}{g}_{\bar{n},s}^{\prime}(k-i)f(k)\Bigr|\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,1)^{2-s}}.

It thus follows that (
5.74
) holds true. By similar considerations, one can prove that when
