\frac{\bar{n}}{K_{1}}\notin\mathbb{N}
, the off-diagonal entries of
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}
decay as (
5.74
): there exist
C>0,\kappa>0
such that for each
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
,

(5.75)

|(\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M})_{i,j}|\leq\frac{Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{1+d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)^{2-s}}.

Step 4: distortion

For
\alpha\geq\frac{1}{2}
, let
\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{M}_{\bar{n}}(\mathbb{R})
be as in (
5.35
). The argument proceeds by multiplying Equation (
5.65
) by
\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{A}
. Set
\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}=\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\psi
, which solves

(5.76)

\beta\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}+(\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{A}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}-\mathsf{A})\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}+\mathsf{A}\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}=\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{A}(\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})).

Let
\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}=\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}-\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}
. Taking the scalar product of (
5.76
) with
\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}
and integrating over
\nu
yields

(5.77)

\beta\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot(\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}+\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}})\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}+\sum_{i,k}\mathsf{A}_{i,k}\nabla\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot\nabla\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}+\sum_{i,k}(\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{A}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}-\mathsf{A})_{i,k}\nabla\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot\nabla\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}\Bigr]\\
=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}\psi_{i_{0}}+\lambda\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\psi\cdot\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{A}(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})].

Step 5: control on differential commutator

Since
\mathsf{A}
fails to be uniformly positive-definite in
n
, one cannot directly bound the differential term in (
5.77
) by
|D\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}|
. However as we have seen in Lemma
5.5
, the gradient of
\psi
satisfies a global decay estimate whenever
\psi
does. Let us first split the quantity of interest into

(5.78)

\sum_{k}(\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{A}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}-\mathsf{A})_{i,k}\nabla\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot\nabla\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}=\sum_{k}f(i-k)\Bigr(\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{\alpha}}-1\Bigr)\nabla\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot\nabla\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}\\
=\underbrace{\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(k,i)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}f(i-k)\Bigr(\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{\alpha}}-1\Bigr)\nabla\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot\nabla\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}}_{({I})_{i}}\\
+\underbrace{\sum_{{k:d_{\bar{n}}(k,i)>\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}}f(i-k)\Bigr(\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\alpha}}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{\alpha}}-1\Bigr)\nabla\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot\nabla\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}}_{({II})_{i}}.

One seeks to control the expectation of
({I})_{i}
and
({II})_{i}
with respect to
\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{\gamma}D\psi|^{2}]
for some well-chosen constant
\gamma>0
. Fix
\gamma\geq\frac{1}{2}
. Using (
5.75
) and the fact that
\sum_{k=1}^{\bar{n}}\psi_{k}=0
, the second term of (
5.78
) may be bounded by

(5.79)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|({II})_{i}|]\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2-s-\alpha}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(k,i)>\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{2\gamma}|\nabla\psi_{k}|^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\times\Bigr(\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{2\gamma}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}-s-\alpha+\gamma}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{\gamma}D\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For the first term, using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality one can first write

|({I})_{i}|\leq\frac{C}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\Bigr(\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{2\gamma}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)}|\nabla\psi_{k}|^{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr(\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\frac{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{2(\alpha-\gamma)}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{1-2s}}|\nabla\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Summing this over
i
yields

(5.80)

\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}|({I})_{i}|\leq C\Bigr(\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)}d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{2\gamma}|\nabla\psi_{k}|^{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\times\Bigr(\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2-4(\alpha-\gamma)}}\sum_{k:d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,k)^{1-2s}}d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{2\gamma}|\nabla\psi_{k}|^{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2\gamma}|\nabla\psi_{i}|^{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr(\sum_{k=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{2\gamma}|\nabla\psi_{k}|^{2}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(k,i_{0})^{2-2s-4(\alpha-\gamma)}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining (
5.80
) and (
5.79
), one can see that if
\alpha\leq\gamma+\frac{1-s}{2}
, then

(5.81)

\Bigr|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i,k}(\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\mathsf{A}\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}^{-1}-\mathsf{A})_{i,k}\nabla\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot\nabla\psi_{k}^{\mathrm{dis}}\Bigr]\Bigr|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2\gamma}|\nabla\psi_{i}|^{2}\Bigr].

Step 6: control on the commutator
\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}

One should now control the commutator
\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}
appearing in (
5.77
). Let us recall the decay estimate on
f*g_{\bar{n},s}
stated in (
5.74
). By analyzing
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
, one can see that the off-diagonal entries of
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}
typically decay in

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M})_{i,j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)^{2-s}}.

As a consequence Lemma
4.1
tells us that for
\alpha\in(0,\frac{3}{2}-s]
,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}\cdot\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}}^{(1)}\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}]\leq\frac{n^{-\kappa_{0}\varepsilon}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}]+C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

From the positivity of
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}
stated in (
5.73
) this gives

(5.82)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}(\mathsf{A}\mathsf{M}+\delta_{\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}})\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}]\geq\frac{n^{-\kappa_{0}\varepsilon}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}]-n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{\mathrm{dis}}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Step 7: conclusion

Combining (
5.77
), (
5.81
) and (
5.82
) one gets that for
\alpha\in(0,\frac{3}{2}-s]
,

(5.83)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{\alpha}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+n^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}+s}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2(\alpha-\frac{1-s}{2})}|\nabla\psi_{i}|^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr).

In particular taking
\alpha=\frac{3}{2}-s
, one obtains

(5.84)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+n\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2(1-\frac{s}{2})}|\nabla\psi_{i}|^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr).

Furthermore applying the estimate (
5.45
) with
\gamma=1-\frac{s}{2}
, we recognize

(5.85)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2(1-\frac{s}{2})}|\nabla\psi_{i}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq Cn^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(n^{-\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]+n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1-s}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{s}{2}}\\
+n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}n\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]\Bigr).

Since
s\in(0,1)
, combining (
5.84
) and (
5.85
) one gets

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1-s/2}D\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}(n^{\kappa\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+n^{-\varepsilon_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+n\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}).

Taking
\varepsilon_{0}>0
large enough with respect to
\varepsilon
, one obtains the existence of a constant
\kappa>0
such that

(5.86)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{3/2-s}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1-s/2}D\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+n\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}).

Using the expression (
3.10
), one can also see that

(5.87)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon-1}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathrm{L}_{1/2}\psi|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since
\frac{3}{2}-s>\frac{1}{2}
, one gets from (
5.86
) and (
5.87
) the estimates (
5.66
) and (
5.67
).
∎

One shall extend the global decay estimate of Lemma
5.6
to the H.-S. equation without linear constraint.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
s\in(0,1)
. Let
\nu
and
\mathsf{M}
satisfying Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
. Let
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}(\nu)
,
i_{0}\in\bar{I}
and
\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

(5.88)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}\psi+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

There exist a constant
C(\beta)
locally uniform in
\beta
and
\kappa>0
such that

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2-s}|\nabla\psi_{i}|^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{3-2s}\psi_{i}^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

Let
\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of (
5.88
). One can decompose
\psi
into
\psi=v+w
where
v,w\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
solve

(5.89)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}v+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}v=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
v\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})=0&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
v\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}),\end{array}\right.

(5.90)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}w+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}w=\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
w\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

For the vector-field
v
, one may apply Lemma
5.6
which gives

(5.91)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2-s}|\nabla v_{i}|^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\sum_{i=1}^{\bar{n}}d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{3-2s}v_{i}^{2}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

as well as

(5.92)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\lambda^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{1-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

It remains to address Equation (
5.90
). One can write a mean-field approximation for (
5.90
) in the form
f(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})
where
f\in H^{1}(\nu)
is the solution of

(5.93)

\beta f+\frac{1}{\bar{n}^{1-s}}\mathcal{L}^{\nu}f=\lambda.

By integration by parts this implies together with the control (
5.92
) that

(5.94)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[f^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{2-s-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.95)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla f|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{\frac{3}{2}-s-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Define
w^{(1)}=f\times(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})
and
w^{(2)}=w-w^{(1)}
which is solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}w^{(2)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}w^{(2)}=-\beta\mathsf{M}^{(2)}w^{(1)}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
w\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

By (
5.94
), there holds

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\mathsf{M}^{(2)}w^{(1)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{\frac{3}{2}-s-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In particular, we have

(5.96)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|w|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{\frac{3}{2}-s-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

and similarly

(5.97)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla w|]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{1-\frac{s}{2}-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

It follows from (
5.96
) and (
5.97
) that
w
satisfies the estimate (
5.91
) and so does
\psi
.
∎

5.5.
Localization and optimal decay

Let us now adapt the localization argument of Subsection
4.4
to derive the near-optimal decay of the solution of (
5.65
). Having proved Lemma
5.6
, it remains to control the decay of
\psi_{j}
for a single
j\in\bar{I}
. To this end, we project the periodized equation (
5.65
) into a small window centered around
j
. After isolating an exterior field, one can see that the projected equation has a similar structure as the equation one is starting from. By splitting the external field in a suitable manner, one can then decompose the solution into two parts, that we control separately.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
s\in(0,1)
. Let
\nu
and
\mathsf{M}
satisfying Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
. Let
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}(\nu)
,
i_{0}\in\bar{I}
and

\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

(5.98)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}\psi+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}})=0&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

There exist
C(\beta)
locally uniform in
\beta
and
\kappa>0
such that for each
1\leq i\leq n
,

(5.99)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi_{i}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{1+d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{2-s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.100)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla\psi_{i}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{1+d_{\bar{n}}(i,i_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

We proceed by bootstrapping the decay exponent on solutions of (
5.98
) and (
5.88
) for
all

\mathsf{M}
satisfying Assumptions
5.2
. Assume that there exist
\alpha\geq\frac{3}{2}-s
and
\gamma\geq 1-\frac{s}{2}
with
\gamma\leq\alpha
such that for
\mathsf{M}
satisfying Assumptions
5.2
and all
\chi_{n}\in H^{-1}(\nu)
,
i_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
, if
\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
solves (
5.98
) or (
5.88
), then there exists
C(\beta)
and
\kappa>0
such that for each
1\leq j\leq\bar{n}
,

(5.101)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\alpha}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.102)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla\psi_{j}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\gamma}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In addition to (
5.101
) and (
5.102
), we will also make a systematic use of the global estimates of Lemma
5.6
and Lemma
5.7
.

Step 1: projection and embedding

Let
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}(\nu)
,
i_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\psi\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of (
5.98
). Fix an index
j\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
and define the window

(5.103)

J:=\{i\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}:d_{\bar{n}}(i,j)\leq d_{\bar{n}}(i_{0},j)/2\}.

Let
n_{0}=|J|
. Let
\psi^{J}:=(\psi_{i})_{i\in J}\in L^{2}(J,H^{1}(\nu))
. Projecting (
5.98
) onto (
5.103
) reads

(5.104)

\begin{cases}\beta\mathsf{M}^{J}{\psi^{J}}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi^{0}=-\beta\Bigr(\sum_{l\in J^{c}}\mathsf{M}_{i,l}\psi_{l}\Bigr)_{i\in J}&\text{on $\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})$}\\
{\psi^{0}}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on $\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})$}.\end{cases}

Let us operate the series of reductions of Subsection
5.1
to reduce the study to a periodic system of size
\bar{n}_{0}=\lfloor n_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{s}}\rfloor
. One may assume that
d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})\geq n^{\kappa\varepsilon}
for some large
\kappa>0
, otherwise the statements (
5.99
) and (
5.100
) are straightforward. Let us denote
\bar{J}=\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}_{0}\}
. We now let
d
stand for the symmetric distance on
\bar{J}
. Consider the Riesz matrix on
\bar{J}
truncated at
K_{0}=\lfloor n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\rfloor
chosen as in (
5.2
), namely
\mathsf{M}_{0}=\nabla^{2}F(x)\in\mathcal{M}_{\bar{n}_{0}}
for some
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}_{0}}
where

F:X_{\bar{n}_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}_{0}}\mapsto\sum_{i,j\in\bar{J}:d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(i,j)\geq K_{0}}g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}^{\prime\prime}(j-i)(x_{i}+\ldots+x_{j})^{2}.

Consider the block decomposition of
\mathrm{H}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n_{0}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\bar{n}_{0}-n_{0}}
,

(5.105)

\mathrm{H}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{0}&B_{0}\\
C_{0}&D_{0}\end{pmatrix},\quad A_{0}\in\mathcal{M}_{n_{0}}(\mathbb{R}).

Let us add and subtract to the first line of (
5.104
) the quantity
B_{0}(D_{0}+\beta^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{n_{0}})C_{0}
. Defining

\mathsf{M}_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{M}^{J}&B_{0}\\
C_{0}&D_{0}\end{pmatrix},

with
B_{0}
,
C_{0}
and
D_{0}
as in (
5.105
), this allows one to identify
{\psi}^{J}_{j}
with
\psi^{0}_{j}
for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,n_{0}\}
, where
\psi^{0}\in L^{2}(\bar{J},H^{1}(\nu))
solves

\begin{cases}\beta{\mathsf{M}}_{0}\psi^{0}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\psi^{0}={\mathrm{V}}&\text{on $\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})$}\\
\psi^{0}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on $\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})$}.\end{cases}

Moreover, the external field
{\mathrm{V}}\in L^{2}(\bar{J},H^{1}(\nu))
satisfies
{\mathrm{V}}_{l}=0
if
l\in\{n_{0}+1,\ldots,\bar{n}_{0}\}
and for each
l\in\{1,\ldots,n_{0}\}
,

{\mathrm{V}}_{l}=-\beta\sum_{i\in J^{c}}\mathsf{M}_{i,l}\psi_{i}-\sum_{i\in J}e_{l}\cdot B_{0}(\beta D_{0}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\otimes I_{\bar{n}_{0}-n_{0}})^{-1}(C_{0}e_{i}\psi_{i})+\lambda.

Note that
\mathsf{M}_{0}
satisfies Assumptions
5.2
.

Step 2: splitting of the exterior potential

Fix
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
and let us partition
\bar{J}
into
K:=\lfloor d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\rfloor
intervals
I_{1},\ldots,I_{K}
of equal size up to a
O(d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}})
. For each
k\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
, let
i_{k}
be an index in the center of
I_{k}
. One can split the external potential into
\mathrm{V}=\mathrm{V}^{(1)}+\mathrm{V}^{(2)}
, where

\mathrm{V}^{(2)}_{l}=\mathrm{V}_{i_{k}}\quad\text{if $l\in I_{k}$}.

Note that
\mathrm{V}^{(2)}
is piecewise constant on the partition
\bar{J}=\cup_{k=1}^{K}I_{k}
. By linearity,
\psi^{0}
can be decomposed into
\psi^{0}=v+w
with
v,w\in L^{2}(\bar{J},H^{1}(\nu))
solving

(5.106)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}_{0}v+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}v=\sum_{l\in J}\mathrm{V}_{l}^{(1)}e_{l}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
v\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}),\end{array}\right.

(5.107)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}_{0}w+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}w=\sum_{l\in J}\mathrm{V}^{(2)}_{l}e_{l}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
w\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Step 3: study of
v

By using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Equation (
5.24
), the fact that
\sum_{k=1}^{\bar{n}}\psi_{k}=0
, the estimates (
5.66
) and (
5.67
) and Lemma
5.2
, one may check that for each
l\in J
,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\mathrm{V}^{(1)})_{l}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\frac{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,l)^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}-s}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial J)^{\frac{1}{2}+s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Note that we have not made use of the bootstrap assumption for this last estimate but rather of the global estimate (
5.66
). Let us decompose
v
into
v=\sum_{l\in J}v^{(l)}
where for each
l\in J
,
v^{(l)}\in L^{2}(\bar{J},H^{1}(\nu))
solves

(5.108)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}_{0}v^{(l)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}v^{(l)}=\mathrm{V}_{l}^{(1)}e_{l}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
v^{(l)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}),\end{array}\right.

By applying the bootstrap assumption (
5.101
) in the window
\bar{J}
, one can see that for each
l\in J
and
j\in\bar{J}
,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(v_{j}^{(l)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\frac{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,l)^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}-\alpha}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}-s}}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(l,\partial J)^{\frac{1}{2}+s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Summing this over
l\in J
yields

(5.109)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[v_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\alpha+\varepsilon^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In a similar manner, using the induction hypothesis (
5.102
), one also obtains

(5.110)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla v_{j}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\gamma+\varepsilon^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Step 4: study of
w

It remains to study the solution
w
associated to the piecewise constant vector-field
\mathrm{V}^{(2)}
. We will construct an approximation of
w
by replacing
\mathsf{M}_{0}
by the constant Riesz matrix on the window
\bar{J}
. For each
k\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
, let
w^{(k)}\in L^{2}(\bar{J},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}_{0}w^{(k)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}w^{(k)}=\mathrm{V}^{(2)}_{i_{k}}\sum_{l\in I_{k}}e_{l}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
w^{(k)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\end{array}\right.

and
\phi^{(k)}\in L^{2}(\bar{J},H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of

(5.111)

\beta g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}*\phi^{(k)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\phi^{(k)}=\mathrm{V}^{(2)}_{i_{k}}\sum_{l\in I_{k}}e_{l}.

Let also
\mathsf{M}_{0}^{(2)}
be the difference between
\mathsf{M}_{0}
and the Toeplitz matrix associated to
g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}
. Finally set
\eta^{(k)}\in L^{2}(\bar{J},H^{1}(\nu))
defined for each
i\in\bar{J}
by
\eta^{(k)}_{i}=\phi^{(k)}_{i+1}-\phi^{(k)}_{i}
. One can observe that

\beta g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}*\eta^{(k)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\eta^{(k)}=\mathrm{V}^{(2)}_{i_{k}}(e_{i_{k+1}}-e_{i_{k}}).

In view of Lemma
5.6
, there holds

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\mathrm{V}_{i_{k}}^{(2)})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Besides, from the global estimate of Lemma
5.6
, letting
S=g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}*\phi^{(k)}
, we have

(5.112)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[S_{i}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

One may then write
\phi^{(k)}_{j}
as

\phi_{j}^{(k)}=\sum_{l\in\bar{J}}g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}^{-1}(j-l)S_{l}=\underbrace{\sum_{l\in\bar{J}:d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,l)>\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})}g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}^{-1}(j-l)S_{l}}_{({I})_{j}}+\underbrace{\sum_{l\in\bar{J}:d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,l)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})}g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}^{-1}(j-l)S_{l}}_{({II})_{j}}.

For the first term, employing (
5.112
), we find

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[({I})_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,i_{0})d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})^{1-s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

One may then split the second term into

(5.113)

({II})_{j}=\underbrace{\sum_{l\in\bar{J}:d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,l)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})}g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}^{-1}(j-l)(S_{j}-S_{l})}_{({II})_{j}^{\prime}}+\underbrace{\sum_{l\in\bar{J}:d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,l)\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})}g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}^{-1}(j-l)S_{j}}_{({II})_{j}^{\prime\prime}}.

In view of (
5.112
),
({II})_{j}^{\prime\prime}
is bounded by

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(({II})^{\prime\prime}_{j})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})^{1-s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For
({II})_{j}^{\prime}
we can note that

(5.114)

S_{l}-S_{j}=\sum_{i\in\bar{J}}\phi_{i}^{(k)}\Bigr(g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}(l-i)-g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}(j-i)\Bigr)=\sum_{i\in\bar{J}}(\phi_{i}^{(k)}-\phi_{j}^{(k)})\Bigr(g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}(l-i)-g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}(j-i)\Bigr).

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the global decay estimate of Lemma
5.6
shows there exist
C(\beta)>0,\kappa>0
such that for each
i,j\in\bar{I}
,

(5.115)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\phi_{i}^{(k)}-\phi_{j}^{(k)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\sum_{l\in(i,j)}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(l,\partial I_{k})^{3-2s}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Again, one can split the sum (
5.114
) according to whether
d(i,j)\leq 3d(l,j)
. For the first contribution we find using (
5.115
),

\Bigr|\sum_{i\in\bar{J}:d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(i,j)\leq 3d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(l,j)}(\phi_{i}^{(k)}-\phi_{j}^{(k)})\Bigr(g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}(l-i)-g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}(j-i)\Bigr)\Bigr|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\frac{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,l)^{1-s}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})^{1-s}}.

Regarding the second contribution we find

\Bigr|\sum_{i\in\bar{J}:d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(i,j)\geq 3d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(l,j)}(\phi_{i}^{(k)}-\phi_{j}^{(k)})\Bigr(g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}(l-i)-g_{\bar{n}_{0},s}(j-i)\Bigr)\Bigr|\\
\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})}\sum_{i\in\bar{J}:d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(i,j)\geq 3d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(l,j)}\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(i,\partial I_{k})^{1-s}}\frac{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,l)}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,i)^{1+s}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,l)}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})}.

Assembling the above leads to

(5.116)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\phi_{j}^{(k)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})^{1-s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

A similar computation shows that

(5.117)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla\phi_{j}^{(k)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Step 5: conclusion for
\mathsf{M}_{0}^{(2)}=0

Assume that
\mathsf{M}_{0}^{(2)}=0
. Then
\phi^{(k)}=w^{(k)}
for each
k\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
. Summing (
5.109
) and (
5.116
) over
k
and choosing
\varepsilon^{\prime}
small enough shows that there exists a small
\eta>0
such that

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\alpha+\eta}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{2-s}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla\psi_{j}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\gamma+\eta}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

One concludes after a finite number of steps that

(5.118)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{2-s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.119)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla\psi_{j}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Step 6: control of
w
in the general case

Let us go back to the general case and define
e^{(k)}=w^{(k)}-\phi^{(k)}
where
\phi^{(k)}
is as in (
5.111
). Note that
e^{(k)}
solves

\beta\mathsf{M}_{0}e^{(k)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}e^{(k)}=-\beta\mathsf{M}_{0}^{(2)}\phi^{(k)}.

According to the estimates (
5.116
) and (
5.117
) of Step 4, the vector-field
\mathsf{M}_{0}^{(2)}\phi^{(k)}
satisfies for each
1\leq i\leq n
,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[((\mathsf{M}_{0}^{(2)}\phi)_{i}^{(k)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,i_{0})d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(i,\partial I_{k})^{1-s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla(\mathsf{M}_{0}^{(2)}\phi)_{i}^{(k)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,i_{0})d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(i,\partial I_{k})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

It follows from the bootstrap assumptions (
5.101
) and (
5.102
) that for each
1\leq i\leq n
,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(e_{i}^{(k)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(i,\partial I_{k})^{2-s}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(i,\partial I_{k})^{\alpha+1-s}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla e_{i}^{(k)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}_{0}}(j,\partial I_{k})^{\gamma+1-s}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Consequently the same estimate holds for
w^{(k)}
. Summing this over
k
yields this existence of a constant
\kappa>0
such that

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime})}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{2-s}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\alpha+1-s}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla w_{j}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime})}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\gamma+1-s}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combined with (
5.109
) and (
5.110
), this improves the induction hypotheses (
5.101
) and (
5.102
) provided
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
is chosen small enough. After a finite number of iterations, one finally gets (
5.99
) and (
5.100
).

Step 7: conclusion for equation (
5.88
)

In view of the bootstrap assumption, it remains to consider the solution
\psi
of (
5.88
). Let us split
\psi
as in the proof of Lemma
5.7
into
\psi=v+w
where
v,w\in L^{2}(\bar{I},H^{1}(\nu))
are solutions of (
5.89
) and (
5.90
). By applying the result of Step 6 to
v
, one can see that there exists a positive
\eta>0
such that for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
,

(5.120)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[v_{i}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\alpha+\eta}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{2-s}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.121)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla v_{i}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\gamma+\eta}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

As in the proof of Lemma
5.7
one shall split
w
into
w=w^{(1)}+w^{(2)}
with

w^{(1)}=f(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{\bar{n}}),

where
f
is given by (
5.93
). Let
\mathsf{M}^{(2)}
be the difference between
\mathsf{M}
and the Toeplitz matrix associated to
g_{\bar{n},s}
. Observe that
w^{(2)}
solves

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}w^{(2)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}w^{(2)}=-\beta\mathsf{M}^{(2)}w^{(1)}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
w\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Using (
5.94
) we find that for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\mathsf{M}^{(2)}w^{(1)})_{i}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{2-s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Employing the bootstrap assumption to bound
w^{(2)}
, we find that for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{n}\}
,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(w^{(2)}_{i})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{2-s}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\alpha+1-s}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Similarly, applying (
5.95
), one gets

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla w^{(1)}_{i}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}}+\frac{1}{d_{\bar{n}}(j,i_{0})^{\gamma+1-s}}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining the two last displays with (
5.120
) and (
5.121
) improves the recursion hypothesis when
\psi
is solution of (
5.88
).
∎

Remark 5.4
.

Even though the Lagrange multiplier in (
5.98
) is of order
1/n
, there is no correction of order
1/n
in (
5.99
), contrarily to the case
s>1
. This is related to the fact that
u:=\mathbb{H}_{n,s}^{-1}(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{n})
satisfies
u_{i}\sim c/n^{1-s}
for each
1\leq i\leq n
. Note that in the above proof, the Lagrange multiplier is contained in
\mathrm{V}^{(2)}
and the smallness of the associated solution shown in (
5.116
).

5.6.
Decay estimate for solutions of (
5.1
)

In the case
n\leq N/2
, one shall now deduce from Proposition
5.8
a control on the solution of (
5.1
).

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
s\in(0,1)
. Let
i_{0}\in\{\frac{n}{4},\ldots,\frac{3n}{4}\}
,
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}(\nu)
and
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
solution of

(5.122)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A_{1}^{\nu}\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})),\end{array}\right.

There exist constants
C(\beta)>0,c(\beta)>0,\delta>0
and
\kappa>0
such that for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,

(5.123)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{1}{|j-i_{0}|^{2-s}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr)(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\chi_{n}|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}).

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Proposition
4.5
. Denote
d
the usual distance on
\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Let
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu))
be the solution of (
5.122
) and
\psi^{(1)}
solution of

(5.124)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu}\psi^{(1)}=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi^{(1)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}),\end{array}\right.

Let
\psi^{(2)}:=\psi-\psi^{(1)}
, which solves

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A_{1}^{\nu}\psi^{(2)}=-\beta M\psi^{(1)}&\text{on }\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})\\
\psi^{(2)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}).\end{array}\right.

Taking the scalar product of the above equation with
\psi^{(2)}
and integrating by parts under
\nu
yields

(5.125)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{(2)}|^{2}]\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi^{(2)}\cdot M\psi^{(1)}].

We claim that uniformly in
1\leq j\leq n
,

(5.126)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\psi^{(2)}\cdot M\psi^{(1)}]\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{1-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{(2)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the good event (
5.6
). Fix
1\leq j\leq n
. One can split the quantity
(M\psi^{(1)})\cdot e_{j}
into

(M\psi^{(1)})\cdot e_{j}=\underbrace{\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)\leq n/4}e_{j}\cdot M(e_{k}\psi_{k}^{(1)})}_{({I})_{j}}+\underbrace{\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)>n/4}e_{j}\cdot M(e_{k}\psi_{k}^{(1)})}_{({II})_{j}}.

By (
5.33
) and (
5.34
),
one may bound the first quantity by

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}\psi^{(2)}_{j}({I})_{j}]\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)\leq n/4}\frac{1}{d(k,i_{0})^{2-s}}\frac{1}{d(k,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\psi_{j}^{(2)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq\frac{C(\beta)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\psi_{j}^{(2)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{n^{1-\frac{s}{2}-\kappa\varepsilon}d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For the second quantity, we can write

(5.127)

({I})_{j}=\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)>n/4}e_{j}\cdot M((e_{k}-e_{i_{0}})\psi^{(1)}_{k})+\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)\leq n/4}e_{j}\cdot M(e_{i_{0}}\psi_{k}^{(1)}).

For the first term of the last display, using the bound on the increments of
M
given in (
6.12
), we find that

(5.128)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}\psi_{j}^{(2)}e_{j}\cdot\Bigr(\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)>n/4}M((e_{k}-e_{i_{0}})\psi^{(1)}_{k})\Bigr)\Bigr]\\
\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)>n/4}\frac{1}{d(i_{0},k)^{1-s}}\frac{1}{n^{1+\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\psi_{j}^{(2)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\psi_{j}^{(2)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{n^{1-\frac{s}{2}+\kappa\varepsilon}d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Because
\psi^{(1)}\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{n})=0
, the second term of (
5.127
) satisfies

(5.129)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\Bigr[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}\psi^{(2)}_{j}e_{j}\cdot\Bigr(\sum_{k:d(k,\partial I)>n/4}M(e_{i_{0}}\psi_{k}^{(1)})\Bigr)\Bigr]\leq\frac{C(\beta)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\psi_{j}^{(2)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{n^{1-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Putting (
5.127
), (
5.128
) and (
5.129
) together we obtain (
5.126
). Summing this over
j
yields

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}\psi^{(2)}\cdot M\psi^{(1)}]\leq\frac{C(\beta)\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{(2)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{n^{1-\kappa\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}].

Finally, inserting the maximum principle of Proposition
3.6
we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}\psi^{(2)}\cdot M\psi^{(1)}]\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\sup|\chi_{n}|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{(2)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}|M|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\sup|\chi_{n}|\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{(2)}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Inserting the last displays into (
5.125
) we find

(5.130)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\psi^{(2)}|^{2}]\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{1-\kappa\varepsilon}}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]+e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\sup|\chi_{n}|^{2}).

In particular, for each
1\leq j\leq n
, there holds

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(\psi^{(2)}_{j})^{2}]\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{1-\kappa\varepsilon}}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\chi_{n}^{2}]+e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\sup|\chi_{n}|^{2})

and the estimate (
5.123
) follows.
∎

5.7.
Proof of Theorem
1

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
2
, one may deduce Theorem
1
from the decay estimate of Proposition
5.9
.
∎

6.
Uniqueness of the limiting measure

In this section we show that the sequence of the laws of microscopic processes converges, in a suitable topology, to a certain point process
\mathrm{Riesz}_{s,\beta}
, as claimed in Theorem
3
. The existence of an accumulation point being a routine argument, Theorem
3
is in fact a uniqueness result. To establish the uniqueness of the accumulation point, we demonstrate that microscopic point processes form, in an appropriate sense, a Cauchy sequence. In the following subsection, we further explain the strategy of proof and show how the problem can be reduced to a correlation question.

6.1.
Reduction to a correlation estimate

In the present section one seeks to compare the two following quantities:

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[F(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[F(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})],\quad\text{with}\quad F:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}\quad\text{smooth},

where
1\leq n\leq N\leq N^{\prime}
. Let us denote
I=\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\pi:\mathcal{M}_{N}\to\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})
the projection on the coordinates
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
. We claim that if
F
depends on variables in the bulk of
\{1,\ldots,n\}
, then the expectations of
F
under
\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}
approximately coincide whenever
N
and
N^{\prime}
are chosen large enough. We will draw an exterior configuration
y=(y_{n+1},\ldots,y_{N})\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
from
\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}
and an exterior configuration
z=(z_{n+1},\ldots,z_{N^{\prime}})\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N^{\prime}})
from
\mathbb{P}_{N^{\prime},\beta}
and compare the conditioned measures
\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}(\cdot\mid y)
and
\mathbb{P}_{N^{\prime},\beta}(\cdot\mid z)
. Let us slightly modify the measures
\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}
and
\mathbb{P}_{N^{\prime},\beta}
by adding the following quantity to the Hamiltonian:

(6.1)

\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}:X_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta(n^{-\varepsilon}x_{i})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta(n^{-\varepsilon}x_{i}^{-1}).

Define the constrained measures

(6.2)

\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\propto e^{-\beta\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi}\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}},\quad\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\propto e^{-\beta\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi}\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}.

We say that a configuration
y=(y_{n+1},\ldots,y_{N})\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
is admissible if

(6.3)

|y_{i}+\ldots+y_{i+k}-k|\leq Cn^{\varepsilon}k^{\frac{s}{2}}\quad\text{for each $n+1\leq i,i+k\leq N$}

and that
y\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
and
z\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N^{\prime}})
are compatible if

(6.4)

N-(y_{n+1}+\ldots+y_{N})=N^{\prime}-(z_{n+1}+\ldots+z_{N^{\prime}}).

Given
y\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
and
z\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N^{\prime}})
two admissible and compatible configurations, denote

(6.5)

\nu_{n}^{y}=\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid y)\quad\text{and}\quad\nu_{n}^{z}=\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid z).

Letting

(6.6)

\mathcal{A}_{n}=\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N}):x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}\leq N-y_{n+1}-\ldots-y_{N}\},

we can write

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\nu_{n}^{y}(x)

\displaystyle\propto e^{-\beta(\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}(x)+\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,y)+\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}(x))}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\nu_{n}^{z}(x)

\displaystyle\propto e^{-\beta(\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}(x)+\mathcal{H}_{n,N^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,z)+\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}(x))}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}(x)\mathrm{d}x,

where
\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,y)
, defined in (
5.5
), stands for the interaction between
x
and
y
. A first possibility to compare
\nu_{n}^{y}
and
\nu_{n}^{z}
is to transport one measure onto the other and to study the decay of the solution of the Monge-Ampere equation. Instead, we interpolate between
\nu_{n}^{y}
and
\nu_{n}^{z}
and consider a continuous path
\nu(t)
in the space of probability measures on
\pi(\mathcal{M}_{N})
. There are several ways of interpolating, one of them consisting in running the Langevin dynamics as in
[
AW22
]
. Alternatively, one can consider a convex combination of
\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{n,N^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{g}}
. For
t\in[0,1]
, define

E(t)(x)=(1-t)\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,y)+t\mathcal{H}_{n,N^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,z)\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}(t)=\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}+\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}+{E}(t)

and the probability measure

(6.7)

\mathrm{d}\nu(t)(x)\propto e^{-\beta{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{g}}_{n}(t)(x)}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}(x)\mathrm{d}x.

Observe that
\nu(0)=\nu_{n}^{y}
and
\nu(1)=\nu_{n}^{z}
.

Let
G:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
be a measurable bounded function. Define

h:t\in[0,1]\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[G].

It is straightforward to check that
h
is smooth and that for all
t\in(0,1)
,

h^{\prime}(t)=\beta\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu(t)}[G,\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,y)-\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,z)].

Integrating this between
0
and
1
, we obtain the following integral representation of the difference of the expectations of
G
under
\nu_{n}^{y}
and
\nu_{n}^{z}
:

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
G:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
be a measurable bounded function. Let also
\nu(t)
be the measure defined in (
6.7
). We have

(6.8)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{n}^{z}}[G]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{n}^{y}}[G]\\
+\beta\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu(t)}[G,\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,y)-\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,z)]\mathrm{d}t.

We will consider functions
G
depending on a small number of coordinates in the bulk of
\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Let us emphasize that
\partial_{i}(\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,y)-\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,z))
typically decays in
{d_{n}(i,\partial I)^{-\frac{s}{2}}}
under
\nu(t)
. One should therefore prove that the decay of correlations under
\nu(t)
is fast enough in order to compensate the long-range nature of the interaction and conclude that the covariance term in (
6.8
) is small. In order to apply the result of Proposition
4.5
to the measure
\nu(t)
, one shall first prove some rigidity estimates under
\nu(t)
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
s\in(0,1)
. Let
1\leq n\leq N\leq N^{\prime}
with
N\gg n^{\frac{2}{s}}
. Let
y\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
and
z\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N^{\prime}})
be two admissible and compatible configurations in the sense of (
6.3
) and (
6.4
). Let
\nu(t)
be the probability measure (
6.7
). There exist constants
\kappa>0
,
C(\beta)>0
and
c(\beta)>0
locally uniform in
\beta
such that

(6.9)

\displaystyle\nu(t)(n^{-\kappa\varepsilon}\leq x_{i}\leq n^{\kappa\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\geq 1-C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}},\quad\text{for each $1\leq i\leq n$},

(6.10)

\displaystyle\nu(t)(|x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}-k|\geq n^{\kappa\varepsilon}k^{\frac{s}{2}})

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}},\quad\text{for each $1\leq i\leq i+k\leq n$}.

Lemma
6.2
, whose proof is given in Appendix
D
, implies that
\nu(t)
verifies Assumptions
5.1
. Let us now split the operator
A_{1}^{\nu(t)}
in a convenient way in order to simplify the analysis of the correlations equation. Following Subsection
5.1
, one can decompose
A_{1}^{\nu(t)}
into
A_{1}^{\nu(t)}=\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu(t)}+M(t)
with

\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu(t)}:=\beta\nabla^{2}\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}+\beta A\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}+\beta\nabla^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}(x)+E(t))\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}\\
-\beta B(D+\beta^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{\nu(t)}\otimes I_{n})^{-1}C+\mathcal{L}^{\nu(t)}\otimes I_{n},

M(t):\beta\nabla^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}+E(t))\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}-\beta A\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}+\beta B(D+\beta^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{\nu(t)}\otimes I_{n})^{-1}C,

where
A,B,C,D
are as in (
5.2
) and
\mathcal{A}
as in (
5.6
). Denote
d
the usual distance on
\{1,\ldots,n\}
. In view of Lemmas
6.2
and
5.2
, for
s\in(0,1)
, there exist
C(\beta)>0,c(\beta)>0,\delta>0
and
\kappa>0
such that for each
1\leq i,j,l\leq n
,
\eta
and all
\phi\in L^{2}(\nu(t))
,

(6.11)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[(\phi e_{j})\cdot M(t)(\eta e_{i})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(i,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\phi^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\eta^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\sup|\phi|\sup|\eta|,

(6.12)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}\Bigr[(\phi e_{j})M(t)(\eta(e_{i}-e_{l}))\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}d(i,l)}{\min(d(i,\partial I)^{1+\frac{s}{2}},d(j,\partial I)^{1+\frac{s}{2}})d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\phi^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\eta^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
+C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\sup|\phi|\sup|\eta|.

Similarly in the case
s\in(1,+\infty)
, there exist
C(\beta)>0,c(\beta)>0,\delta>0
and
\kappa>0
such that for each
1\leq i,j,l\leq n
and all
\eta
,
\phi\in L^{2}(\nu(t))
,

(6.13)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[(\phi e_{j})\cdot M(t)(\eta e_{i})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(i,\partial I)^{s-\frac{1}{2}}d(j,\partial I)^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\phi^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\eta^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\sup|\phi|\sup|\eta|,

(6.14)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}\Bigr[(\phi e_{j})M(t)(\eta(e_{i}-e_{l}))\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}d(i,l)}{\min(d(i,\partial I)^{\frac{3}{2}+s},d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{3}{2}+s})d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{1}{2}+s}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\phi^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\eta^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
+C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\sup|\phi|\sup|\eta|.

6.2.
Estimate on the main equation

Our goal is to study the decay of the solution
\psi
of the Helffer-Sjöstrand equation associated to
\nu(t)
when the source vector-field is localized on a small number of coordinates in the center of
I
. Since
\nu(t)
verifies Assumptions
5.1
, one may apply the result of Proposition
5.8
to
\bar{A}_{1}^{\nu(t)}
. By convexity, this yields a satisfactory bound on
\psi
, thus proving that that the correlation between a given gap in the center of
I
and the interaction energy
{E}(t)
, tends to
0
as
n
tends to infinity.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
s\in(0,1)\cup(1,+\infty)
. Let
y\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
be an admissible configuration in the sense of (
6.3
) and
\nu(t)
be the measure defined in (
6.7
). Let
\chi_{n}\in H^{1}
,
i_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
such that
|i_{0}-\frac{n}{2}|\leq\frac{n}{4}
. Let
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu(t)))
solving

(6.15)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\nabla^{2}({\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}(t)+\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}})\psi+\mathcal{L}^{\nu(t)}\psi=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\mathcal{A}_{n}\\
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\mathcal{A}_{n}.\end{array}\right.

Denote
d
the usual distance on
\{1,\ldots,n\}
. There exist constants
C(\beta)>0,\kappa>0
such that

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\psi_{j}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\chi_{n}|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}})(n^{-\frac{s}{2}}\mathds{1}_{s\in(0,1)}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathds{1}_{s\in(1,+\infty)}).

Proof.

Let
s\in(0,1)
. Let
\psi\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu(t)))
be the solution of (
6.15
). Let
\psi^{(1)}\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu(t)))
be the solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\mathsf{M}\psi^{(1)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu(t)}\psi^{(1)}=\chi_{n}e_{i_{0}}&\text{on }\mathcal{A}_{n}\\
\psi^{(1)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\mathcal{A}_{n}.\end{array}\right.

Set
\psi^{(2)}=\psi-\psi^{(1)}\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu(t)))
. One can observe that
\psi^{(2)}
is solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta\nabla^{2}({\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{g}}(t)+\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}})\psi^{(2)}+\mathcal{L}^{\nu(t)}\psi^{(2)}=-\beta M(t)\psi^{(1)}&\text{on }\mathcal{A}_{n}\\
\psi^{(2)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\mathcal{A}_{n}.\end{array}\right.

Since
\nu(t)
satisfies Assumptions
5.1
if
s\in(0,1)
(resp. Assumptions
4.1
if
s\in(1,+\infty)
), one may apply the estimate (
5.99
) of Proposition
5.8
(resp. the estimate (
4.29
) of Proposition
4.3
) to
\psi^{(1)}
which yields

\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[(\psi_{i}^{(1)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\Bigr(\frac{\mathds{1}_{s\in(0,1)}}{d(i,i_{0})^{2-s}}+\frac{\mathds{1}_{s\in(1,+\infty)}}{d(i,i_{0})^{s}}\Bigr).

Together with the bounds (
6.11
) and (
6.12
), this implies

\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[|\psi^{(2)}|^{2}]\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{n^{1-\kappa\varepsilon}}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\chi_{n}^{2}]+\sup|\chi_{n}|^{2}e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}).

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, this yields

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{d(j,\partial I)^{\frac{s}{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[(\psi_{j}^{(2)})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon-\frac{s}{2}}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\chi_{n}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\chi_{n}|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}})

and the same estimate holds for
\psi
. We conclude likewise in the case
s\in(1,+\infty)
.
∎

6.3.
Proof of Theorem
3
and Theorem
4

By inserting the decay estimate of Lemma
6.3
into the identity (
6.8
), one may easily compare the measures
\nu_{n}^{y}
and
\nu_{n}^{z}
when the exterior configurations
y
and
z
are compatible. After integrating
y
and
z
in the set of admissible and compatible configurations, there remains to show that typical fluctuations of
x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}
do not affect much the measures, thus demonstrating the following comparison inequality:

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
s\in(0,1)\cup(1,+\infty)
. Let
G:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
in
H^{1}
such that
\sup|\nabla G|<\infty
. Assume that
G
depends only on the variables
x_{i}
for
i\in J:=\{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor-K,\ldots,\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor+K\}
with
K\leq n/4
. Let
\mathcal{A}
be the good event (
5.6
). We have

(6.16)

\Bigr|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})]\Bigr|\\
\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}(n^{-\frac{\min(s,1-s)}{2}}\mathds{1}_{s\in(0,1)}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathds{1}_{s\in(1,+\infty)})\Bigr(\sum_{i\in J}\sup_{\mathcal{A}}|\partial_{i}G|+\sum_{i\in J}\sup|\partial_{i}G|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\Bigr).

Proof.

Let
G:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying the above. Let
\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}
be the forcing (
6.1
) and
\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}
,
\mathbb{Q}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}
be the measures (
6.2
). The measure
\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi^{-1}
being uniformly log-concave with constant
c=\beta n^{-\varepsilon(s+2)}
one may apply the Bakry-Emery to see that

\mathrm{Ent}[\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi^{-1}\mid\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi^{-1}]\leq 2c^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[|\nabla\mathrm{F}^{\mathrm{g}}|^{2}].

Using in turn the rigidity estimates of Lemma
2.1
and Theorem
6
and the Pinsker inequality, one can write

(6.17)

\mathrm{TV}(\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi^{-1},\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi^{-1})\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}.

By similar arguments one also has

\mathrm{TV}(\mathbb{P}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi^{-1},\mathbb{Q}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\pi^{-1})\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}.

Let
y\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
and
z\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N^{\prime}})
be two admissible configurations in the sense of (
6.3
). Assume that
y
and
z
are compatible in the sense of (
6.4
), i.e
N-(y_{n+1}+\ldots+y_{N})=N^{\prime}-(z_{n+1}+\ldots+z_{N^{\prime}})
. Denote
\mathcal{A}_{n}
the domain (
6.6
),
\nu_{n}^{y}
and
\nu_{n}^{z}
the measures (
6.5
) and
\nu(t)=(\nu(t,y,z))
the interpolating measure (
6.7
). For each
i\in J
, let
\psi^{(t,i)}\in L^{2}(I,H^{1}(\nu(t)))
be the solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A_{1}^{\nu(t)}\psi^{(t,i)}=(\partial_{i}G)e_{i}&\text{on $\mathcal{A}_{n}$}\\
\psi^{(t,i)}\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on $\partial\mathcal{A}_{n}$}.\end{array}\right.

By applying the estimates of Lemmas
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.3
, we find

(6.18)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid{y}]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid{z}|&\leq\int_{0}^{1}|\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu(t)}[G,\mathcal{H}_{n,N^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{g}}-\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}]|\mathrm{d}t\\
&=\sum_{i\in J}\int_{0}^{1}|\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[\nabla(\mathcal{H}_{n,N^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{g}}-\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}})\cdot\psi^{(t,i)}]|\mathrm{d}t\\
&\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon-\frac{\min(s,1)}{2}}\sum_{i\in J}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[(\partial_{i}G)^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup|\partial_{i}G|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}})\\
&\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon-\frac{\min(s,1)}{2}}\Bigr(\sum_{i\in J}\sup_{\mathcal{A}}|\partial_{i}G|+\sum_{i\in J}\sup|\partial_{i}G|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\Bigr),\end{split}

where we have used the fact that the event (
5.6
) has overwhelming probability under
\nu(t)
. Since
y
(resp.
z
) is admissible under
\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}
(resp.
\mathbb{Q}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}
) with overwhelming probability, we have by integrating (
6.18
) that for
\alpha_{n}\in(n-n^{\varepsilon+\frac{\min(s,1)}{2}},n+n^{\varepsilon+\frac{\min(s,1)}{2}})
,

(6.19)

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=\alpha_{n}]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=\alpha_{n}]\\
+O_{\beta}\Bigr(n^{\kappa\varepsilon-\frac{s}{2}}\sum_{i\in J}\sup_{\mathcal{A}}|\partial_{i}G|+\sum_{i\in J}\sup|\partial_{i}G|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\Bigr).

We have thus compared the measures
\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{N^{\prime},\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}
when
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
are constrained to occupy the same volume. Our aim is now to show that making
\alpha_{n}
slightly vary does not affect much the expectation of
G
. Let us upper bound the quantity

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=\alpha_{n}]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=n].

Set
\alpha=\frac{\alpha_{n}}{n}
. Performing the change of variables
\Phi(\alpha,\cdot):X_{N}\mapsto X_{N}^{\prime}
with

\begin{cases}x_{i}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\alpha}x_{i}&\text{if $i\in I$}\\
x_{i}^{\prime}=\frac{N-n}{N-\alpha n}x_{i}&\text{if $i\in I^{c}$}\end{cases}

reads

(6.20)

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=n\alpha]=\alpha^{-n}\Bigr(\frac{N-n}{N-\alpha n}\Bigr)^{N-n}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\alpha}}[G\circ\pi\circ\Phi(\alpha,\cdot)\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=n],

where
\mu_{\alpha}:=\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}\circ\Phi^{-1}
, which may be expressed in the form

\mathrm{d}\mu_{\alpha}(x)\propto e^{-\beta H(\alpha,x)}\mathrm{d}x.

Differentiating (
6.20
) with respect to
\alpha
gives

\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\alpha}}[G\circ\pi\circ\Phi(\alpha,\cdot)\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=n]=\beta\mathrm{Cov}_{\mu_{\alpha}}[G\circ\pi\circ\Phi(\alpha,\cdot),\partial_{1}H]+\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\alpha}}\Bigr[\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha}\nabla\Phi(\alpha,\cdot)\cdot\nabla(G\circ\pi)\circ\Phi(\alpha,\cdot)\Bigr].

Using a rough upper bound on the variance of the energy
\partial_{1}H
, one gets the existence of constants
C(\beta)>0,\kappa>0
such that uniformly in
\alpha
,

(6.21)

\Bigr|\frac{\partial}{\partial\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=n\alpha]\Bigr|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon+\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr(\sum_{i\in J}\sup_{\mathcal{A}}|\partial_{i}G|+\sum_{i\in J}\sup|\partial_{i}G|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\Bigr).

Since
\alpha=O(n^{\frac{s-1}{2}+\varepsilon})
for
s\in(0,1)
one may deduce by Taylor expansion that

\Bigr|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=\alpha_{n}]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=n]\Bigr|\\
\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon+\frac{s-1}{2}}\Bigr(\sum_{i\in J}\sup_{\mathcal{A}}|\partial_{i}G|+\sum_{i\in J}\sup|\partial_{i}G|e^{-c(\beta)n^{\delta}}\Bigr),

which tends to
0
as
N
tends to infinity. When
s\in(1,+\infty)
it is easy to refine (
6.21
). Applying Proposition
4.5
to the measure
\mu_{\alpha}
, one obtains since
\alpha=O(n^{-1/2+\varepsilon})
,

\Bigr|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=\alpha_{n}]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[G\circ\pi\mid x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}=n]\Bigr|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon-\frac{1}{2}}.

In combination with (
6.19
) this finishes the proof of (
6.16
).
∎

We are now ready to conclude the proof of the uniqueness of the limiting measure. We will consider random variables in the space of configurations on
\mathbb{R}
and one should first define a
\sigma
-algebra on it. We let
\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R})
be the set of locally finite and simple point configurations in
\mathbb{R}
. Given a Borel set
B\subset\mathbb{R}
, we denote
N_{B}:\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{N}
the number of points lying in
B
. We then endow
\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R})
with the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the maps
\{N_{B}:B\ \text{Borel}\}
. We call point process a probability measure on
\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R})
. We then say that a sequence
P_{N}
of point processes converges to
P
for the local topology on
\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R})
whenever for any bounded, Borel and local function
f:\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
, the following convergence holds:

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{P_{N}}[f]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[f].

Proof of Theorems
3
and
4
.

Step 1: compactness.

Let
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{N})
distributed according to
\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}
. Denote

Q^{N}=\mathrm{Law}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{Nx_{i}}\mathds{1}_{|x_{i}|<\frac{1}{4}}\right)\in\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R})).

Let us show that the sequence
(Q^{N})
has an accumulation point in the local topology on
\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R}))
. We follow the strategy of
[
DHLM21
, Prop. 2.9]
. For all
R>0
denote
\Lambda_{R}=[-R,R]
and for all
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R}))
,
Q_{R}
the law of
\mathcal{C}|_{\Lambda_{R}}
when
\mathcal{C}
is distributed according to
Q.
For two point processes
P
and
Q
, define the relative specific entropy of
P
with respect to
Q
by

\mathrm{Ent}[P\mid Q]=\limsup_{R\to\infty}\mathrm{Ent}[P_{R}\mid Q_{R}].

Let
\Pi
be a Poisson point process on
\mathbb{R}
. According to
[
GZ93
, Prop. 2.6]
, the level sets of
\mathrm{Ent}[\cdot\mid\Pi]
are sequentially compact for the local topology. As a consequence it is enough to check that

(6.22)

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\sup_{K\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\frac{1}{K}\mathrm{Ent}[Q^{N}_{K},\Pi_{\Lambda_{K}}]<\infty.

Let
B_{K,\Lambda_{K}}
be a Bernoulli process on
\Lambda_{K}
. Following
[
DHLM21
]
, one can split the relative entropy into

(6.23)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Ent}[Q^{N}_{K}\mid\Pi_{\Lambda_{K}}]&=\int\log\frac{\mathrm{d}Q_{K}^{N}}{\mathrm{d}B_{K,\lambda_{K}}}\mathrm{d}Q_{K}^{N}+\int\log\frac{\mathrm{d}B_{K,\Lambda_{K}}}{\mathrm{d}\Pi_{\Lambda_{K}}}\mathrm{d}Q_{K}^{N}\\
&=-\log K_{N,\beta}(\Lambda_{K})-\beta\mathbb{E}_{Q_{K}^{N}}\Bigr[\sum_{x_{i}\neq x_{j}\in\mathcal{C}}g_{s}(x_{i}-x_{j})\Bigr]-\log\left(e^{-N}\frac{N^{N}}{N!}\right),\end{split}

where

K_{N,\beta}(\Lambda_{K})=\int\exp\Bigr(-\beta\sum_{x_{i}\neq x_{j}\in\mathcal{C}\cap\Lambda_{K}}g_{s}(x_{i}-x_{j})\Bigr)\mathds{1}_{\frac{N}{4}D_{N}(X_{N})}\mathrm{d}X_{N}.

From the rigidity estimates of Theorem
6
, we have

\log K_{N,\beta}(\Lambda_{K})=-\beta\mathbb{E}_{Q_{K}^{N}}\Bigr[\sum_{x_{i}\neq x_{j}\in\mathcal{C}\cap\Lambda_{K}}g_{s}(x_{i}-x_{j})\Bigr]+O_{\beta}(K).

Inserting this into (
6.23
), we deduce that (
6.22
) holds. It follows that
(Q^{N})
has an accumulation point in the local topology.

Step 2: uniqueness.

Let us now prove that this accumulation point is unique. Let
P,Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R}))
be two accumulation points of
(Q^{N})
in the local topology. Note that
P
and
Q
are necessarily translation invariant. Let
k_{0}\geq 1
. Set

F:\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Conf}(\mathbb{R})\mapsto G(z_{2}-z_{1},\ldots,z_{k_{0}}-z_{1}),

with
G:\mathbb{R}^{k_{0}}\to\mathbb{R}
smooth. In view of Proposition
6.4
, we can see that

\mathbb{E}_{P}[F]=\mathbb{E}_{Q}[F].

This implies that for each
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
, the law of
(z_{2}-z_{1},\ldots,z_{k_{0}}-z_{1})
under
P
equals the law of
(z_{2}-z_{1},\ldots,z_{k_{0}}-z_{1})
under
Q
. Since
P
and
Q
are translation invariant, we conclude that
P=Q
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
4
is now straightforward.

Proof of Theorem
4
.

By Theorem
3
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}}[F\circ\pi]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Riesz}_{s,\beta}}[G(z_{2}-z_{1},\ldots,z_{k_{0}}-z_{1})].

Since the error term in (
6.16
) is uniform in
N
, this concludes the proof of Theorem
3
.
∎

6.4.
Proof of the hyperuniformity result

Having already established in
[
Bou21
]
that the
N
-Riesz gas is hyperuniform and that
N(x_{K}-x_{1})
is of order
O(K^{s})
under
\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}
with a Gaussian asymptotic behavior, it is now immediate using the convergence result of Theorem
3
to prove that
\mathrm{Riesz}_{s,\beta}
is also hyperuniform.

Proof of Theorem
5
.

Let
1\leq K\leq\frac{N}{2}
. Set
\ell_{N}=\frac{N}{K}
. Let

F_{N}=(N\ell_{N})^{-\frac{s}{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathds{1}_{(0,\ell_{N})(x_{i})}-\ell_{N}\right).

Let
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
with

\sigma^{2}=\frac{1}{\beta\frac{\pi}{2}s}\mathrm{cotan}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}s\right).

Let
\eta:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
|\eta^{\prime}|_{\infty}\leq 1
. In
[
Bou21
]
, we have proved that

(6.24)

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}}[\eta(F_{N})]=\mathbb{E}[\eta(Z)]+o_{K}(1),

with a
o_{K}(1)
uniform in
N
. Set

\widetilde{F}_{N}=K^{-\frac{s}{2}}N(x_{K}-x_{1}).

Using Theorem
6
, we can prove that

(6.25)

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}}[\eta(\widetilde{F}_{N})]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}}[\eta(F_{N})]+o_{K}(1),

with a
o_{K}(1)
uniform in
N
. Now by Theorem
4
, we have

(6.26)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}}[\eta(\widetilde{F}_{N})]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Riesz}_{s,\beta}}[\eta(K^{-\frac{s}{2}}(z_{K}-z_{1}-K))].

Combining (
6.24
), (
6.25
) and (
6.26
), one deduces that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Riesz}_{s,\beta}}[\eta(K^{-\frac{s}{2}}(z_{K}-z_{1}-K))]=\mathbb{E}[\eta(Z)]+o_{K}(1).

We deduce that under the process
\mathrm{Riesz}_{s,\beta}
, the sequence
K^{-\frac{s}{2}}(z_{K}-z_{1}-K)
converges in distribution to
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
. Moreover by
[
Bou21
]
,

\mathrm{Var}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}}[F_{N}]=\mathrm{Var}[Z]+o_{N}(K^{s}),

with a
o_{N}(K^{s})
uniform in
N
. Proceeding as above, one easily prove the variance estimate (
1.9
).
∎

6.5.
Proof of the repulsion estimate

Proof of Proposition
1.1
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,\frac{s}{2})
. It was proved in
[
Bou21
, Lem. 4.5]
that there exist constants
C(\beta)>0,c(\beta)>0
locally uniform in
\beta
such that for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
\varepsilon>0
small enough,

\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}(N(x_{i+1}-x_{i})\leq\varepsilon)\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)\varepsilon^{-\alpha}}.

Since
(\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}})
converges to
\mathrm{Riesz}_{s,\beta}
in the local topology, one can pass the above inequality to the limit which gives

\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{Riesz}_{s,\beta}}(z_{i+1}-z_{i}\leq\varepsilon)\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)\varepsilon^{-\alpha}}.

∎

Appendix A
Discrete Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality

The Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, originally proved independently in
[
Gag58
,
Nir59
]
, is an interpolation inequality between different weak derivatives in
L^{p}
spaces. The result was at first stated for derivatives of integer order and then extended to derivatives of fractional order in the rather recent paper
[
BM18
]
. The main result of
[
BM18
]
gives sufficient and necessary conditions on the orders and exponents for an interpolation inequality to hold on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For shortcut, we only present one of the cases where the interpolation inequality is valid.

Lemma A.1

(Brezis-Mironescu)
.

Let
1\leq p,p_{1},p_{2}\leq\infty
. Let
s_{1},s_{2}\geq 0
and
\theta\in(0,1)
such that

s_{1}\leq s_{2},\quad s_{0}=\theta s_{1}+(1-\theta)s_{2},\quad\frac{1}{p}=\frac{\theta}{p_{1}}+\frac{1-\theta}{p_{2}}.

Assume that
s_{2}<1
. Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
p_{1},p_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\theta
such that for all
u\in W^{s_{1},p_{1}}(\mathbb{R})\cap W^{s_{2},p_{2}}(\mathbb{R})
,

\|u\|_{W^{s_{0},p}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\|u\|_{W^{s_{1},p_{1}}(\mathbb{R})}^{\theta}\|u\|_{W^{s_{2},p_{2}}(\mathbb{R})}^{1-\theta}.

By taking a periodic function of period
1
on
(-n,n)
, one can show by letting
n
tend to infinity that Lemma
A.1
also holds for functions defined on the circle.

Appendix B
Well-posedness results

The proofs of Propositions
3.1
and
3.3
can be found in
[
Bou21
, App. A]
. For completeness we sketch the main arguments below.

Let
\mu
satisfying Assumptions
3.1
. The formal adjoint with respect to
\mu
of the derivation
\partial_{i}
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
is given by

\partial_{i}^{*}w=\partial_{i}w-(\partial_{i}H)w,

meaning that for all
v,w\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(D_{N},\mathbb{R})
such that
\nabla w\cdot\vec{n}=0
, the following identity holds

(B.1)

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\partial_{i}v)w]=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[v\partial_{i}^{*}w].

The above identity can be shown by integration by parts under the Lebesgue measure on
D_{N}
. Recall the map

\Pi:X_{N}\in D_{N}\mapsto(x_{2}-x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}-x_{1})\in\mathbb{T}^{N-1}

and

\mu^{\prime}=\mu\circ\Pi^{-1}.

Proof of Proposition
3.1
.

Let
F=G\circ\Pi
with
G\in H^{1}(\mu)
. Recall that if
F\in H^{1}(\mu)
, then
\nabla F\in L^{2}(\{1,\ldots,N\},H^{-1}(\mu))
. Let

E=\{\phi\circ\Pi:\phi\in H^{1}(\mu^{\prime}),\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\phi\circ\Pi]=0\}.

Consider the functional

J:\phi\in E\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[|\nabla\phi|^{2}-2\phi F].

One may easily check that
J
admits a unique minimizer. Indeed for all
\phi=\psi\circ\Pi\in E
, one can write

|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\phi F]|\leq\|F\|_{H^{-1}(\mu)}|\|\phi\|_{H^{1}(\mu)}.

Moreover since
\phi\in E
, one can observe that

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[|\phi|^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\prime}}[|\psi|^{2}]\leq c^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\prime}}[|\nabla\psi|^{2}]=\frac{1}{2c}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[|\nabla\phi|^{2}].

It follows that
J
is bounded from below. Since
J
is convex and l.s.c, by standard arguments, it is l.s.c for the weak topology of
H^{1}(\mu)
and therefore
J
admits a minimizer
\phi
.

One can then easily check by integration by parts that the Euler-Lagrange equations for
\phi
state that a.e on
D_{N}
,

(B.2)

\mathcal{L}^{\mu}\phi=F-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[F],

with the boundary condition

(B.3)

\nabla\phi\cdot\vec{n}=0,

a.e on
\partial D_{N}
. Equations (
B.2
) and (
B.3
) easily imply that
J
admits a unique minimizer.

Let us now differentiate rigorously Equation (
B.2
). Let
w\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(D_{N})
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
. By integration by parts, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[w\partial_{i}F]=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\partial_{i}^{*}w(F-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[F])]=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\partial_{i}^{*}w\mathcal{L}\phi]=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\nabla\partial_{i}^{*}w\cdot\nabla\phi]\\
=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\partial_{i}^{*}\partial_{j}w)\partial_{j}\phi]+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\left[\partial_{j},\partial_{i}^{*}\right]w)\partial_{j}\phi].

The first term of the right-hand side of the last display may be expressed as

\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\partial_{i}^{*}\partial_{j}w)\partial_{j}\phi]=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\partial_{j}w)\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi]=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\nabla w\cdot\nabla(\partial_{i}\phi)]=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[w\mathcal{L}^{\mu}(\partial_{i}\phi)].

For the second term, recalling the identity
\left[\partial_{j},\partial_{i}^{*}\right]=(\nabla^{2}H)_{i,j},
one may write

\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\left[\partial_{j},\partial_{i}^{*}\right]w)\partial_{j}\phi]=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(w\cdot\nabla^{2}H\nabla\phi)_{i}].

One deduces that, in the sense
H^{-1}(\mu)
, for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
,

(\nabla^{2}H\nabla\phi)_{i}+\mathcal{L}^{\mu}(\partial_{i}\phi)=\partial_{i}F.

Together with the boundary condition (
B.3
), this concludes the proof of existence and uniqueness of a solution to (
3.5
). We turn to the proof the variational characterization of the solution of (
3.5
). Let

(B.4)

J:L^{2}(\{1,\ldots,N\},H^{1}(\mu))\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[|D\psi|^{2}+\psi\cdot\nabla^{2}H\psi-2\psi\cdot\nabla F].

By standard arguments, one can prove that
J
admits a minimizer
\psi
, which satisfies the Euler-Lagrange equation

A_{1}^{\mu}\psi=\nabla F.

Moreover, one may assume that
\psi\cdot\vec{n}=0
on
\partial D_{N}
. By integration by parts, we conclude that
\psi=\nabla\phi
.
∎

Let us now prove Proposition
3.3
. Recall the notation

\mathrm{Gap}_{N}:X_{N}\in D_{N}\mapsto(N|x_{2}-x_{1}|,N|x_{3}-x_{2}|,\ldots,N|x_{N}-x_{1}|)\in\mathbb{R}^{N},

\mathcal{M}_{N}=\mathrm{Gap}_{N}(D_{N})\quad\text{and}\quad\nu=\mathrm{Gap}_{N}\#\mu.

Proof of Proposition
3.3
.

Let
G\in H^{-1}(\nu)
. Denote
E=\{\phi\in H^{1}(\nu):\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\phi]\}=0
and
J
the functional

J:\phi\in E\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[|\nabla\phi|^{2}-2\phi G].

By standard arguments (see the proof of Proposition
3.1
), we can show that
J
admits a unique minimizer
\phi
. Since
\phi
is a minimizer of
J
, for all
h\in E
,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla h]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[Gh].

By integration by parts, one can observe that for all
h\in E
,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla h]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\phi h]+\int_{\partial\mathcal{M}_{N}}(\nabla\phi\cdot\vec{n})he^{-H}.

By density, it then follows that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\phi=G-\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[G]&\text{on }\mathcal{M}_{N}\\
\nabla\phi\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\mathcal{M}_{N}.\end{array}\right.

To prove that
\nabla\phi
satisfies the Helffer-Sjöstrand equation (
3.9
), we need to adapt the integration by parts formula (
B.1
). One may easily show that for all
v\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
such that
\nabla v\cdot\vec{n}=0
on
\partial D_{N}
and
\psi\in L^{2}(\{1,\ldots,N\},\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
such that
\psi\cdot(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{N})=0
, there holds

(B.5)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\left[\psi\cdot\nabla v\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\left[v(-\nabla H^{\mathrm{g}}\cdot\psi+\mathrm{div}\psi)\right].

Let
w\in L^{2}(\{1,\ldots,N\},\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{M}_{N}))
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{N}w_{i}=0
. In view of (
B.5
),

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w\cdot\nabla G]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[(G-\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[G])(-\nabla H^{\mathrm{g}}\cdot w+\mathrm{div}w)]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\phi(-\nabla H^{\mathrm{g}}\cdot w+\mathrm{div}w)].

Integrating part the last equation gives

\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w\cdot\nabla G]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla(-\nabla H^{\mathrm{g}}\cdot w+\mathrm{div}w)]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[w\cdot(\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\nabla\phi+\nabla^{2}H^{\mathrm{g}}\nabla\phi)].

By density, we deduce that there exists a Lagrange multiplier
\lambda\in H^{-1}(\nu)
such that

\nabla^{2}H^{\mathrm{g}}\nabla\phi+\mathcal{L}^{\nu}\nabla\phi=\nabla G+\lambda(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{N}).

Recalling that
\nabla\phi\cdot\vec{n}=0
on
\partial\mathcal{M}_{N}
, this yields the existence of a solution to (
3.9
). Since
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{i}\phi=0
, taking the scalar product of the above equation with
e_{1}+\ldots+e_{N}
yields

\lambda=\frac{1}{N}(e_{1}+\ldots+e_{N})\cdot\nabla^{2}H^{\mathrm{g}}\nabla\phi.

The uniqueness of the solution to (
3.9
) is straightforward. The proof of the variational characterization comes from arguments similar to the proof of Proposition
3.1
.
∎

Appendix C
Local laws for the HS Riesz gas

Lemma C.1
.

Let
s>1
. For all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, there exists
\delta>0
such that

(C.1)

\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}(N(x_{i+1}-x_{i})\geq k^{\varepsilon})\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)k^{\delta}},\quad\text{for each $1\leq i\leq N$}.

For all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, there exists
\delta>0
such that

(C.2)

\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}(|N(x_{i+k}-x_{i})-k|\geq k^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon})\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)k^{\delta}},\quad\text{for each $1\leq i\leq N$ and $1\leq k\leq\frac{N}{2}$}.

Let us sketch the proof of the above lemma.

Sketch of the proof.

We show recursively, starting from
K=N
, that the log-Laplace transform of
N(x_{i+K}-x_{i})
is of order
O(K)
uniformly in
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
. The point is that one may control the interaction between two sub-intervals by simply shrinking the configuration as in
[
HLSS18
, Proof of Prop. 4.4]
. Let us first prove that there exists a constant
C(\beta)>0
such that for any
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
k\geq 1
,

(C.3)

\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}}[\exp(N(x_{i+k}-x_{i})]\leq C(\beta)k.

For clarity we work in zoomed coordinates. For all
R>0
let us denote
\Lambda_{R}=[-\frac{R}{2},\frac{R}{2}]
and
\mathbb{T}_{N}
the torus of size
N
. Let
P_{N}
be the periodic Riesz gas with
N
points in
\mathbb{T}_{N}
:

\mathrm{d}P_{N}(X_{N})\propto e^{-\beta H_{N}(X_{N})}\mathds{1}_{X_{N}\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{N}}\mathrm{d}X_{N},

where

H_{N}:X_{N}\in(\mathbb{T}_{N})^{N}\mapsto\sum_{i\neq j}g_{N,s}(x_{i}-x_{j}).

Let also
Q_{n,R}
be the Riesz gas with
n
points in
\Lambda_{R}
and
K_{n,R}
be the associated partition function:

\mathrm{d}Q_{n,R}(X_{n})=\frac{1}{K_{n,R}}e^{-\beta\tilde{H}_{n}(X_{n})}\mathds{1}_{\Lambda_{R}^{n}}(X_{n})\mathrm{d}X_{n},

where

\tilde{H}_{n}:X_{n}\in\Lambda_{R}^{n}\mapsto\sum_{i\neq j}g_{N,s}(x_{i}-x_{j}).

For any measurable subset
B
in
\mathbb{T}_{N}
or
\Lambda_{R}
and
n\geq 1
denote

\mathcal{N}_{n}(B)=\mathrm{card}\{1\leq i\leq n:x_{i}\in B\}.

We proceed to the proof of (
C.3
) by bootstrap on scales.

Fix
C_{0}>0
. Let
\gamma\in(0,1]
. Assume that for any
N\geq 1,R\geq N\gamma
and
n\geq 1,R^{\prime},R>0
such that
|n-R^{\prime}|\leq\frac{R^{\prime}}{2},R\geq R^{\prime}\gamma
we have

(C.4)

\mathbb{E}_{P_{N}}[e^{\mathcal{N}_{N}(\Lambda_{R})}]\leq C_{0}R,

(C.5)

\mathbb{E}_{Q_{n,R^{\prime}}}[e^{\mathcal{N}_{n}(\Lambda_{R})}]\leq C_{0}R.

Let us prove that (
C.4
) and (
C.5
) hold for some
\gamma^{\prime}
much smaller than
\gamma
for some well-controlled
C_{0}^{\prime}>0
.

Let
R^{\prime}=N\gamma
. For a small parameter
\alpha>0
to be fixed later, let us consider
R\geq\max(R^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}},\sqrt{\gamma}R)
. Let us prove that (
C.4
) holds for the interval
\Lambda_{R}
. Since the bootstrap assumption (
C.4
) holds for
R^{\prime}
, one may restrict the integral to an event where the number of points inside
\Lambda_{R^{\prime}}
is well-controlled. Let
n\geq 1
such that
|n-R^{\prime}|\leq\frac{R^{\prime}}{2}
. One studies

\mathbb{E}_{P_{N}}[e^{\mathcal{N}_{N}(\Lambda_{R})}\mid\mathcal{N}_{N}(\Lambda_{R^{\prime}})=n]=\frac{\int_{\mathbb{T}_{N}^{N}}e^{\mathcal{N}_{N}(\Lambda_{R})-\beta H_{N}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{N}(\Lambda_{R^{\prime}})=n}}{\int_{\mathbb{T}_{N}^{N}}e^{-\beta H_{N}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{N}_{N}(\Lambda_{R^{\prime}})=n}}.

To bound the numerator from above, one can simply use the fact that the interaction between
\Lambda_{R^{\prime}}
and
\Lambda_{R^{\prime}}^{c}
is positive:

(C.6)

\int_{\mathbb{T}_{N}^{N}}e^{\mathcal{N}_{N}(\Lambda_{R})-\beta H_{N}}\mathds{1}_{N(\Lambda_{R^{\prime}})=n}\leq K_{N-n,N-R^{\prime}}\times\int_{\Lambda_{R^{\prime}}^{n}}e^{\mathcal{N}_{n}(\Lambda_{R})-\beta\tilde{H}_{n}}

To bound the denominator from below, one can condition on the fact that all points
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
lie in a smaller window
\Lambda^{\prime}=[-\frac{R^{\prime}}{2}+R^{1-\varepsilon},\frac{R^{\prime}}{2}-R^{1-\varepsilon}]\subset\Lambda_{R^{\prime}}
. Denote

\mathcal{A}=(\Lambda^{\prime})^{n}.

Let us then partition
\mathbb{T}_{N}\setminus\Lambda_{R^{\prime}}
into
m:=\lfloor\frac{N-n}{R^{\prime}}\rfloor+1
intervals
Q_{k},k=1,\ldots,m
of approximate sizes
R^{\prime}
. On the event
\mathcal{A}
, one can bound from below the interaction between
\Lambda_{R^{\prime}}
and
\Lambda_{R^{\prime}}^{c}
, denoted
H(\Lambda_{R^{\prime}},\Lambda_{R^{\prime}}^{c})
, by

(C.7)

H(\Lambda_{R^{\prime}},\Lambda_{R^{\prime}}^{c})\geq-\frac{R^{2(1+\alpha)}}{R^{s}}-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{N-n}(Q_{k})}{(kR^{\prime})^{s}}

Fix
\varepsilon>0
and
\alpha=\frac{s-1-\varepsilon}{2}>0
, so that by Jensen’s inequality, one may write from (
C.7
),

\log\int_{\mathbb{T}_{N}^{N}}e^{-\beta H_{N}}\mathrm{d}X_{N}\geq\log\int_{\mathcal{A}}e^{-\beta\tilde{H}_{n}}+\log K_{N-n,N-R^{\prime}}-\beta\log\mathbb{E}_{Q_{N-n,N-R^{\prime}}}\Bigr[\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{N-n}(Q_{k})}{(kR^{\prime})^{s}}\Bigr]-O(R^{1-\varepsilon}).

Applying the bootstrap assumption (
C.5
) to
Q_{N-n,N-R^{\prime}}
, we find

\log\mathbb{E}_{Q_{N-n,N-R^{\prime}}}\Bigr[\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{N-n}(Q_{k})}{(kR^{\prime})^{s}}\Bigr]=O(R^{1-\varepsilon}).

One then gets by scaling that the partition function of the
n
variables conditioned to
\mathcal{A}
satisfies

\log\int_{\mathcal{A}}e^{-\beta\tilde{H}_{n}}=\log K_{n,R^{\prime}}+O(R^{1-\varepsilon}).

It thus follows that

(C.8)

\log\int_{\mathbb{T}_{N}^{N}}e^{-\beta H_{N}}\mathrm{d}X_{N}\geq\log K_{n,R^{\prime}}+\log K_{N-n,N-R^{\prime}}+O(R^{1-\varepsilon}).

Assembling (
C.6
) and (
C.8
) shows that

\log\mathbb{E}_{P_{N}}[e^{\mathcal{N}_{N}(\Lambda_{R})}\mid\mathcal{N}_{N}(\Lambda_{R^{\prime}})=n]=\log\mathbb{E}_{Q_{n,R^{\prime}}}[e^{\mathcal{N}_{n}(\Lambda_{R})}]+O(R^{1-\varepsilon}).

Note

\frac{R}{R^{\prime}}=\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{\gamma}\geq\gamma,

since
\gamma^{\prime}\geq\sqrt{\gamma}
. One can therefore apply the bootstrap assumption (
C.5
) to
Q_{n,R^{\prime}}
and
\Lambda_{R}
to deduce that

\log\mathbb{E}_{Q_{n,R^{\prime}}}[e^{\mathcal{N}_{n}(\Lambda_{R})}]\leq C_{0}R.

Finally applying the induction hypothesis (
C.4
) to control the probability that
|\mathcal{N}_{N}(\Lambda_{R^{\prime}})-R^{\prime}|\geq\frac{R^{\prime}}{2}
one obtains
