\log\mathbb{E}_{P_{N}}[e^{\mathcal{N}_{N}(\Lambda_{R})}]\leq C_{0}R+O(R^{1-\varepsilon}).

We obtain by a similar proof that for any
n\geq 1,R^{\prime}>1
such that
|n-R^{\prime}|\leq\frac{n}{2}
and
R
such that
R\geq\max(N\sqrt{\gamma},(R^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}})
,

\log\mathbb{E}_{Q_{n,R^{\prime}}}[e^{\mathcal{N}_{n}(\Lambda_{R})}]\leq C_{0}R+O(R^{1-\varepsilon}),

thus proving the bootstrap assumptions (
C.4
) and (
C.5
) for
C_{0}^{\prime}=C_{0}+O(R^{-\varepsilon})
. Since the initialization is evident we conclude after
O(\log N)
iterations that (
C.3
) holds for some constant
C>0
independent of
R
.

Let us then improve (
C.3
) into (
C.2
). Let
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,\frac{N}{2}\}
,
I
be the window
I=\{i-k,\ldots,i+k\}
,
\theta:\mathbb{R}^{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a smooth cutoff function such that
\theta(x)=x^{2}
if
x>1
,
\theta=0
on
[0,\frac{1}{2}]
and
\theta^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Given
\varepsilon>0
, define

\mathrm{F}=\sum_{i\in I}\theta\Bigr(\frac{N(x_{i+1}-x_{i})}{k^{\varepsilon}}\Bigr)

and
\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}
the constrained measure

\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}\propto e^{-\beta\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}.

From (
C.3
), one gets using the Pinsker and Log-Sobolev inequalities that the total variation distance between
\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}
decays exponentially in
k
, meaning that there exist
\delta>0
depending on
\varepsilon
and
C(\beta),c(\beta)>0
such that

(C.9)

\mathrm{TV}(\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta},\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta})\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)k^{\delta}}.

Moreover, since
\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}
is uniformly log-concave in gap coordinates, one gets the existence of some
\kappa>0
such that for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}}[tN(x_{i+k}-x_{i})]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}}[N(x_{i+k}-x_{i})]=O_{\beta}(k^{1+\kappa\varepsilon}).

Employing (
C.9
) again to now compare the expectations of
N(x_{i+k}-x_{i})
under
\mathbb{P}_{N,\beta}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}
, one concludes the proof of (
C.2
).
∎

Appendix D
Local laws for the interpolating measure

We prove the rigidity estimate of Lemma
6.2
for the conditioned measures (
6.5
) and the interpolating path (
6.7
). The proof is based on an argument of
[
BEY12
, Sec. 3]
.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

Let
y\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
and
z\in\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N^{\prime}})
be as in the statement of Lemma
6.2
and
\nu(t)
as in (
6.7
). Let
\mu(t)
be the push-forward of
\nu(t)
by the map
X_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\mapsto(0,x_{1},x_{1}+x_{2}\ldots,x_{1}+\ldots+x_{n})
. The bound (
6.9
) is immediate in view of the forcing (
6.1
). Let us prove (
6.10
).

Step 1: control of the fluctuations

Let
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
k\in\{1,\ldots,N/2\}
such that
1\leq i+k\leq n
. We wish to prove that for
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
large enough with respect to
\varepsilon
, there exists
\delta>0
depending on
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
such that

(D.1)

\mu(t)\Bigr(|N(x_{i+k}-x_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\mu(t)}[N(x_{i+k}-x_{i})]|\geq k^{\frac{s}{2}+\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}\Bigr)\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)k^{\delta}}.

Following
[
BEY12
, Lem. 3.16-3.17]
, one may first decompose
N(x_{i+k}-x_{i})
into a sum of block average statistics. For each
i\in I^{c}
, let
I_{k}(i)
be an interval of integers of cardinal
k+1
such that
i\in I_{k}(i)
and let

x_{i}^{[k]}=\frac{1}{k+1}\sum_{j\in I_{k}(i)}x_{j}.

Let
\alpha=\frac{1}{p}
for a large
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Since
x_{i}^{[0]}=x_{i}
, one can break
N(x_{i}-x_{i}^{[k]})
into

(D.2)

N(x_{i}-x_{i}^{[k]})=\sum_{m=0}^{p-1}N(x_{i}^{[\lfloor k^{m\alpha}\rfloor]}-x_{i}^{[\lfloor k^{(m+1)\alpha}\rfloor]}).

For each
m\in\{0,\ldots,p-1\}
, denote
G_{m}=N(x_{i}^{[\lfloor k^{m\alpha}\rfloor]}-x_{i}^{[\lfloor k^{(m+1)\alpha}\rfloor]})
and
I_{m}=I_{\lfloor k^{(m+1)\alpha}\rfloor}(i)
. Fix
m\in\{0,\ldots,p-1\}
. Let us bound the fluctuations of
G_{m}
. Since
G_{m}
only depends on the variables
x_{i}
with
i
in
I_{m}
and since
\sum_{i\in I_{m}}\partial_{i}G_{m}
=0, one can use the Gaussian concentration result for divergence free test-functions stated in Lemma
3.9
. Let us introduce the coordinates
x=(x_{i})_{i\in I_{m}}
and
y=(x_{i})_{i\in I\setminus I_{m}}
on
\pi(D_{N})
. The measure
\mu(t)
satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
3.9
in the window
I_{m}
. It can indeed be written

\mathrm{d}\mu(t)=e^{-\beta H(x,y)}\mathds{1}_{\pi(D_{N})(x,y)}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\quad\text{with}\quad H(x,y)=H_{1}(x)+H_{2}(x,y),

with
\nabla^{2}H_{2}\geq 0
,
H_{1}
satisfying
\sum_{i\in I_{m}}\partial_{i}H_{1}=0
with

\nabla^{2}H_{1}\geq N^{2}k^{-(m+1)\alpha(s+2-\varepsilon)}.

Lemma
3.9
therefore gives

\begin{split}\log\mathbb{E}_{\mu(t)}[e^{tG_{m}}]&\leq t\mathbb{E}_{\mu(t)}[G_{m}]+\frac{t^{2}}{2\beta}N^{-2}k^{(m+1)\alpha(s+2+\varepsilon)}|I_{m}|^{-1}\sup|\nabla G_{m}|^{2}\\
&\leq t\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[G_{m}]+\frac{t^{2}}{2\beta}k^{\alpha(s+1)+ms\alpha+\varepsilon(s+2)}.\end{split}

We conclude that for
\varepsilon^{\prime}
large enough with respect to
\varepsilon
, there exists
\delta>0
depending on
\varepsilon^{\prime}
such that

\mu(t)(|G_{m}-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[G_{m}]|\geq k^{\frac{s}{2}+\varepsilon^{\prime}})\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)k^{\delta}}.

Inserting this into (
D.2
), one deduces that for
\varepsilon^{\prime}
large enough with respect to
\varepsilon
, there exists
\delta>0
depending on
\varepsilon^{\prime}
such that

(D.3)

\mu(t)(|N(x_{i}-x_{i}^{[k]})-\mathbb{E}_{\mu(t)}[N(x_{i}-x_{i}^{[k]})]|\geq k^{\frac{s}{2}+\varepsilon})\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)k^{\delta}}.

One can finally check that the variable
N(x_{i+k}^{[k]}-x_{i+k})
verifies the same estimate: proceeding as for
G_{m}
with
m=p-1
, we obtain that for
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
large enough with respect to
\varepsilon
, there exists
\delta>0
depending on
\varepsilon^{\prime}
such that

(D.4)

\mu(t)(|N(x_{i}^{[k]}-x_{i+k}^{[k]})-\mathbb{E}_{\mu(t)}[N(x_{i}^{[k]}-x_{i+k}^{[k]})]|\geq k^{\frac{s}{2}+\varepsilon})\leq C(\beta)e^{-c(\beta)k^{\delta}}.

Combining (
D.3
) applied to
i
and
i+k
and (
D.4
), one finally gets the claim (
D.1
).

Step 2: accuracy estimate

Taking a step back in gap coordinates, one shall now control the expectation of
x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}
under
\nu(t)
. By construction we can write

\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}]-\mathbb{E}_{\nu(0)}[x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}]=\beta\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu(s)}[x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k},\mathcal{H}_{n,N^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,z)-\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,y)]\mathrm{d}s.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and using (
D.1
) one can write

(D.5)

|\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}]-\mathbb{E}_{\nu(0)}[x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}]|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}k^{\frac{s}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{Var}_{\nu(s)}[\mathcal{H}_{n,N^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,z)-\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,y)]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}s.

First, recall that there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
x\in N\mathbb{T}
,

(D.6)

|N^{-s}g_{s}(\frac{x}{N})-\tilde{g}_{s}(x)|\leq\frac{C}{N^{s}},

where
\tilde{g}_{s}:Nx\in\mathbb{T}\mapsto\frac{1}{|x|^{s}}
. Let us denote

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}:(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{N-n}\mapsto\sum_{i\in I,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\setminus I}\frac{1}{|x_{i}+\ldots+y_{j}|^{s}},

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n,N^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{g}}:(x,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{N^{\prime}-n}\mapsto\sum_{i\in I,j\in\{1,\ldots,N^{\prime}\}\setminus I}\frac{1}{|x_{j}+\ldots+z_{j}|^{s}}.

To begin the comparison let us restrict the sum as follows:

(D.7)

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n,N^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,z)-\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(x,y)=\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\setminus I}\Bigr(\frac{1}{|x_{i}+\ldots+z_{j}|^{s}}-\frac{1}{|x_{i}+\ldots+y_{j}|^{s}}\Bigr)\\
+\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j\in\{1,\ldots,N^{\prime}\}\setminus\{1,\ldots,N\}}\frac{1}{|x_{i}+\ldots+z_{j}|^{s}}

Fix
k\in I
. By Taylor expansion, one may write

(D.8)

\sum_{j=n+1}^{N/2}\Bigr(\frac{1}{|x_{k}+\ldots+z_{j}|^{s}}-\frac{1}{|x_{k}+\ldots+y_{j}|^{s}}\Bigr)=\sum_{j=n+1}^{N/2}\tilde{g}_{s}^{\prime}(x_{k}+\ldots+y_{j})N(z_{j}-y_{j})+({I})_{k}

with the error term
({I})_{k}
satisfying

(D.9)

\mathrm{Var}_{\nu(s)}[({I})_{k}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\sum_{j=n+1}^{N^{\prime}/2}\frac{|j-n|^{s+\varepsilon^{\prime}}}{|j-k|^{2+s}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(k,\partial I)^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}},

for some
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
. By Taylor expansion again and using Lemma
6.2
, one can write

\mathrm{Var}_{\nu(s)}[\tilde{g}_{s}^{\prime}(x_{k}+\ldots+y_{j})]\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\tilde{g}_{s}^{\prime\prime}(j-k)(n-k)^{s/2+\kappa\varepsilon}.

The leading-order of the right-hand side of (
D.8
) therefore satisfies

(D.10)

\sum_{j=n+1}^{N^{\prime}/2}\tilde{g}_{s}^{\prime}(x_{k}+\ldots+y_{j})(z_{n+1}+\ldots+z_{j}-(y_{n+1}+\ldots+y_{j}))\\
=\sum_{j=n+1}^{N^{\prime}/2}\tilde{g}_{s}^{\prime}(|j-k|)(z_{n+1}+\ldots+z_{j}-(y_{n+1}+\ldots+y_{j}))+({II})_{k}

with

(D.11)

\mathrm{Var}_{\nu(s)}[({II})_{k}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}\sum_{j=n+1}^{N^{\prime}/2}\frac{|j-n|^{s/2}|n-k|^{s/2+\kappa\varepsilon}}{|j-k|^{s+2}}\leq\frac{C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}}{d(k,\partial I)^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}}.

The point is that leading order term in (
D.10
) is constant with respect to
x
and its variance under
\nu(s)
is therefore
0
. It follows that uniformly in
s
,

(D.12)

\mathrm{Var}_{\nu(s)}\Bigr[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{j=n+1}^{N/2}\Bigr(\frac{1}{|x_{k}+\ldots+z_{j}|^{s}}-\frac{1}{|x_{k}+\ldots+y_{j}|^{s}}\Bigr)\Bigr]\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}.

One may proceed similarly for the terms at the left-hand side of
I
and one concludes that (
D.12
) also holds for the first quantity in (
D.7
). It remains to bound the second term in (
D.7
). By assumptions on
z
, one has

(D.13)

\mathrm{Var}_{\nu(s)}\Bigr[\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j\in\{1,\ldots,N^{\prime}\}\setminus\{1,\ldots,N\}}\frac{1}{|x_{i}+\ldots+z_{j}|^{s}}\Bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon+1}N^{-\frac{s}{2}+\varepsilon}.

Combining (
D.6
), (
D.9
), (
D.12
), we find that uniformly in
s
,

\mathrm{Var}_{\nu(s)}[\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,y)-\mathcal{H}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot,z)]\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}.

Inserting this into (
D.5
) one obtains

(D.14)

|\mathbb{E}_{\nu(t)}[x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}]-\mathbb{E}_{\nu(0)}[x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}]|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}k^{\frac{s}{2}+\kappa\varepsilon}.

Let us denote
\mathcal{B}\subset\pi_{I^{c}}(\mathcal{M}_{N})
the set of admissible configurations as defined in (
6.3
). By taking
t=1
and
N=N^{\prime}
, we find that for all
y,z\in\mathcal{B}
,

(D.15)

|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid y)}[x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid z)}[x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}]|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}k^{\frac{s}{2}+\kappa\varepsilon}.

Since by Theorem
6

|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}}[(x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{B}}]-k|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon},

we deduce from (
D.15
) that for all admissible configurations
y\in\mathcal{B}
,

|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{N,\beta}^{\mathrm{g}}(\cdot\mid y)}[x_{i}+\ldots+x_{i+k}]-k|\leq C(\beta)n^{\kappa\varepsilon}k^{\frac{s}{2}+\kappa\varepsilon}.

Inserting this into (
D.14
) concludes the proof of Lemma
6.2
.
∎
